For each declared or introduced subsystem, trace actual execution and data flow from initialization through runtime, persistence, recovery, failure handling, and shutdown; finish incomplete paths rather than calling presence of a scaffold an implementation. Follow the interfaces among brain, Eyes, Ears, diary, Reality, Life Log, rewards, other organs, and all five existing databases, including backward-compatible historical records. Then audit process and model allocations, fixed output/input/context budgets, temporary buffers, truncations, and resource headroom so data and instructions are not silently shortened, while respecting actual hardware limits. Do not add autonomous-behavior safeguards or replace model choices with deterministic substitutes without asking the project owner. Do this **after** the current workload; neither this note nor green CI claims that exhaustive audit has already happened.

### R2-AUD-2026-10-28-016 — Local Whisper transcription received an incompatible 48 kHz stereo WAV

- **Area:** `r2.c` continuous Ears microphone/desktop buffers, explicit local speech-recognition bridge, `docs/local-speech-input.md`.
- **Finding:** Ears correctly captures independent 48 kHz stereo 16-bit PCM, but `r2_ears_listen_cancelable` previously sent that 48 kHz stereo file directly to `whisper-cli`. whisper.cpp's sample WAV reader requires 16 kHz input. A valid RIFF header at the wrong rate cannot be relied on as a working local transcription bridge, even with a working microphone and installed Whisper model.
- **Fix:** preserve both raw continuous Ears streams and histories. After an explicit 1–15 second listening action captures its full interval to a private WAV, convert **only the temporary clip** to 16 kHz mono 16-bit PCM in bounded chunks, with stereo averaging and a 3:1 downsample. Verify the original sample rate/channels/data size before conversion; reject an incomplete file rather than passing corrupted audio to Whisper. Invoke the configured local `whisper-cli` with the compatible file; cleanup removes both temporary WAV files and the transcript. This does not automatically transcribe, analyze, or change input capture.
- **Regression:** `tests/ears_speech_bridge_smoke.py` asserts the converted file reaches the Whisper executable. Compiled `tests/ears_whisper_wav_runtime.c` checks sample rate, channels, bit depth, full clip duration, converted signal values, original capture preservation, and a truncated source.
- **Limit:** even with the correctly normalized WAV, successful actual speech recognition still depends on `R2_WHISPER_MODEL`, the executable, the real PulseAudio/PipeWire devices, and available CPU/memory on the target installation.


### R2-AUD-2026-10-31-017 — Resume interrupted Ears/Watch identity and ownership trace (IN PROGRESS)

- **Inspection basis:** traced every line of `Ears.c` and `Ears.h` as they existed on `work/r2-priority-fixes-from-audit-2026-10-25` (the source immediately before this workload), plus the audio discovery and Watch coordinator in `shell.c` (~1295–1492); audio capture, ring buffers, attention, explicit transcription, start/stop and conversation association in `r2.c` (~7728–7870, ~10950–11815, ~12735–12785); the Life Log event writer and its source-indexing integration in `Log.c` (~870–961, ~1304–1312, ~1832–1865); Ears/Watch/legacy Life Log regressions, and relevant public headers. Followed call-site references across the other production modules to check for users of the changed Ears event type. **This is not a claim that every line of `r2.c`, `shell.c`, `Log.c`, or every repository file has been audited.**
- **Source-identity loss:** Ears had a 512-byte fixed event field despite potentially longer audio source names and file paths; its default device `pactl` reads and microphone enumeration imposed separate fixed buffers. Desktop open explicitly overwrote the real source with `desktop-audio`; sample/close log details duplicated the identifier into a smaller fixed buffer. Life Log independently rejected a source over 512 bytes. **Repair:** Ears now owns exact source strings and returns an independently allocated snapshot released by `r2_ears_release_event`. Discovery uses dynamically sized lines, desktop opens preserve the monitor name, file-decoder shell command allocation scales with quoted path length, source identity is recorded in the dedicated Life Log field, and Life Log no longer rejects a valid identifier only because it exceeded 512 bytes. Source snapshots in conversation association are protected by audio lifecycle locks and explicitly released. Legacy SQLite schema columns are unchanged.
- **Watch capture-ownership race:** a previous status check followed by a separate generic start let Watch record ownership of a preexisting capture opened between those calls, then close someone else's capture on exit. **Repair:** two separate audio capture generations carry monotonic ownership tokens. The claim is assigned under the same lifecycle mutex that opens the stream; Watch's release compares the exact token under that mutex. A manual explicit start transfers ownership from Watch, leaving its stream active. Later Watch cleanup with a stale token is a no-op. Regressions include a compiled synthetic transport test running the actual core audio worker threads and simulating manual takeover and stale-token cleanup.
- **Direct microphone stop latency:** `r2_ears_listen_cancelable` held the microphone lifecycle mutex while collecting a direct one-shot utterance. A stop previously took that same mutex before expressing stop intent. **Partial repair:** unconditional explicit stop now sets an atomic stop-request count before taking the mutex; one-shot capture checks that request before and during capture, aborts its incomplete WAV, and gives the mutex to stop. Added a synthetic 15-second direct listening regression that requests stop during capture and requires prompt termination without Whisper inference. **Known remaining limitation:** a `pa_simple_read` already blocked inside libpulse cannot be interrupted reliably by this cooperative flag; a truly cancellable asynchronous Pulse capture backend is still required. Stop of an independently running worker can likewise wait on a blocking `pa_simple_read`.
- **Test boundaries:** updated source-level Watch assertions to require token-safe APIs rather than the retired generic Watch stop; extended synthetic dual-Ears runtime tests with long real source names and owned snapshots; extended legacy Life Log database test to persist and retrieve a 1,599-byte audio source. Run the branch's GitHub Actions workflow and inspect its exact head status; a commit or a test passing on an earlier head does not verify newer changes.
- **Still open in this same sensory workload:** audio discovery still uses `pactl` subprocesses rather than native PulseAudio/PipeWire metadata queries. Uncorked playback detection and playback PID remain independent queries; 2048-byte `pactl` parsing in those functions remains; the desktop monitor still defaults to the system's default sink rather than the playing stream's actual routed sink; Watch has not yet distinguished transient query failure from confirmed source disappearance; live Pulse stream routing changes are not yet reflected in owned source identity or ring-buffer generations. These are **not marked fixed**. The source-discovery rewrite described in an interrupted previous session was not found in the retrieved branch and must not be inferred from its earlier narrative.
- **Deferred separately:** PDF/book reading through Eyes stays outside this version's scope until the current implementation is complete and fully audited. Hardware/database baselines already verified by the project owner are not silently relabeled unknown; the new code still needs target-account and real-device acceptance when appropriate.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-01-018 — Native audio discovery, routed sink identity, and Watch recovery (IMPLEMENTED; full host acceptance open)

- **Starting point:** `audit/r2-ears-watch-source-lifecycle-2026-10-31` after completed ownership/snapshot repairs, PR #179; `main` untouched. Checked discovery implementation and call sites in `Ears.c` / `Ears.h`, microphone/desktop start, status, token lifecycle and sensory relationships in `r2.c` / `r2.h`, Watch polling, ownership and media selection in `shell.c`, and all invoked build and test paths. This batch does **not** complete the repository-wide every-line manual audit.
- **Prior defect:** three independent text queries used `pactl` / `grep`, including separate playing/PID probes and the default sink. They could disagree across hotplug/routing changes, mistake a transient command failure for absence, and open a monitor that was not the active stream's actual output sink. Fixed read buffers could also fail on long device identifiers.
- **Repair:** added `AudioDiscovery.c` / `AudioDiscovery.h`. A read-only PulseAudio context queries server defaults, physical source metadata (`monitor_of_sink != PA_INVALID_INDEX`), uncorked sink inputs and application PID, then asks PulseAudio for that sink's actual `monitor_source_name`. An explicit manual desktop open may use the default sink if there is no playback. The callback copies metadata into dynamically allocated owned strings. All queries use a finite monotonic 2,500 ms deadline; failures return -1 rather than masquerading as a successful empty snapshot. This is one client-context snapshot across sequential metadata calls, **not** a server-side atomic transaction.
- **Watch integration:** obtains one discovery result each pass; a failed read does not automatically revoke already-owned microphone/desktop streams or existing media display attention. Watch opens the exact discovered microphone/monitor source, compares an active monitor identity under the desktop lifecycle mutex, and stops/reopens only its own token-owned desktop stream when a confirmed playback route changes. Explicit/manual ownership continues to take priority.
- **Build and test integration:** updated `Makefile`, launcher source/header manifest, CI C-link commands, standalone SIGTERM integration compile sources, and source-level wiring checks. Replaced shell-generated `pactl` fixtures with native introspection callback tests and exact-source PCM fixtures; extended ownership tests to use exact selected source APIs. Verified strict production build and full Actions workflow on code commit `2fac52d88575c5f39cfa990dee2895eeb3e2358a` (run `41374582319`, SUCCESS). This is CI on synthetic and isolated fixtures, **not** installed-host Pulse/PipeWire acceptance.
- **Known limits not fixed:** selection presently follows the first uncorked playback sink input; it does not aggregate simultaneous playback routed to different sinks. Real stream routing can change between metadata calls and the subsequent open; the next Watch poll can correct a managed stream. The native discovery call has a bounded timeout but `pa_simple_read` remains an uninterruptible blocking operation if the audio server stops supplying data, even after the cooperative stop flag is raised. A fully cancellable transport and installed-host acceptance are the next audio lifecycle workload. Continue inspecting other organs, database history, and allocation/serialization paths; don't reinterpret a green CI run as proof of exhaustive source inspection.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-01-019 — Replace uninterruptible pa_simple_read with signalable asynchronous capture (CODE / CI COMPLETE; HOST ACCEPTANCE OPEN)

- **Scope actually inspected and changed:** traced live microphone/desktop `r2_ears_open_source`, source-specific open, read, metadata lifecycle, explicit one-shot listening, continuous workers, Watch ownership tokens, both stop paths and terminal shutdown in `Ears.c`, `Ears.h`, `r2.c`, and `r2.h`. Examined the newly authored `AudioCapture.c/.h` from construction through asynchronous connection, callback wakeups, partial-fragment reads, cancellation, failure unwind, and post-reader release. Verified source manifests in `Makefile`, launcher, workflow and standalone SIGTERM integration; inspected and updated synthetic Ears/WAV/Watch tests. **No claim of whole-repository every-line completion.**
- **Root cause:** `pa_simple_read` could block indefinitely without reaching R2's cooperative stop flag. `r2_ears_stop` or `r2_ears_stop_desktop` could then join a capture thread that could not finish. Another stop could be queued behind the same mutex held by one-shot capture. A passing test based on a pa_simple stub that always returned samples would not detect this.
- **Repair:** introduced `AudioCapture.c/.h`. A dedicated `pa_threaded_mainloop` owns an async `pa_context` and `pa_stream`; ready/readable callbacks signal its condition. A read with no PCM waits via `pa_threaded_mainloop_wait` (which releases the Pulse lock). A stop marks the stream cancelled and signals the waiting reader from another thread through `pa_threaded_mainloop_signal`. The reader checks cancellation before further work and exits with `ECANCELED`. Context/stream connect has a 5-second monotonic Pulse timer, with cancellation also waking an in-progress connection. Source metadata and sample rate/format remain unchanged.
- **PCM integrity / ownership:** record chunks may be larger or smaller than R2's requested 1,920 bytes. The stream retains a `pa_stream_peek` fragment and exact byte offset across reads; the fragment is dropped only once fully consumed, rather than truncating its remainder. Sparse Pulse holes are represented by zero PCM of exactly the declared byte length. The capture thread never frees a transport on failure; its lifecycle owner wakes/joins it and then releases the stream, context, main loop, and event resources. Each Ears instance owns its independent transport; source handles are published under a per-instance mutex so a concurrent cancel cannot race a free.
- **Integration:** Ears' low-level cancel is callable before the microphone's higher-level lifecycle lock, waking a direct one-shot listen holding that mutex. Watch/microphone/desktop stops wake the selected capture before joining, retaining the generation-token ownership check; desktop stop can signal an ongoing connection before waiting for its capture mutex, using a protected lazily initialized desktop Ears pointer. Separate owned microphone and desktop rolling PCM histories and cognition/attention rules are not changed.
- **Regression coverage:** `tests/ears_async_capture_cancel_runtime.c` uses a controlled Pulse event-loop double to make a real `r2_audio_capture_read` wait with **no incoming samples**; it starts a separate thread, cancels, observes `ECANCELED` and a prompt join, and separately checks a producer fragment split across two calls with exactly one drop. The existing dual-Ears runtime fixture now injects the new transport API to test independent source and event lifetimes. The existing synthetic Watch lifecycle, direct one-shot cancellation, Whisper conversion, legacy Life Log, strict build and service SIGTERM tests continue to run in CI. Full workflow `41377015124` passed on code commit `7ed2d06db1255e6d92fcbe9998e0e801895dfbed`.
- **Limitations that must stay visible:** synthetic tests and CI cannot prove live target-host PipeWire device behavior or validate the user's installed database/GPU settings; those host baselines were previously supplied by the project owner and are not discarded. Cancellation depends on the libpulse threaded event loop remaining responsive; it is no longer waiting inside `pa_simple_read`. File-based decoding still uses an FFmpeg pipe and is not covered by the asynchronous **live Pulse** transport guarantee. A stop arriving before a lazily created desktop Ears pointer exists can wait for the bounded 5-second handshake rather than immediately signal an object not yet published. Multi-output concurrent playback routing and a complete line-by-line audit of the rest of R2 remain separate open work. **Do not infer all-bugs-fixed or merge the draft PR.**


### R2-AUD-2026-11-01-020 — Cancel stalled FFmpeg file decoder and preserve full file-event lifecycle (CODE / CI COMPLETE; HOST ACCEPTANCE OPEN)

- **Inspected paths:** read the file-open, decoder-process launch, raw PCM read, EOF/error transitions, cancellation, close, shutdown and source journaling in `Ears.c` / `Ears.h`; traced reader and lifecycle ownership expectations into core Ears/Watch stop and direct listening paths in `r2.c`. Manually followed the new `AudioFile.c` / `AudioFile.h` source in full: pipe setup, spawn actions and exact argv, nonblocking reader / blocking producer, eventfd cancellation, poll/read/EOF, exit status, SIGTERM/SIGKILL and waitpid. Checked all invoked C sources in the launcher, Makefile, CI and standalone SIGTERM harness, plus affected source-level and runtime test coverage. **This is NOT a claim that every remaining R2 file or caller has been manually audited.**
- **Root cause removed for this path:** `popen` created an unnecessary shell and gave Ears no owned decoder PID. `fread` could block indefinitely if FFmpeg stopped producing PCM; `pclose` could block waiting on the decoder. The reader previously called `r2_ears_close` on EOF/error, which could release shared source metadata and transport during another caller's observation.
- **New transport:** `AudioFile.c` starts the exact `ffmpeg` binary through `posix_spawnp` with one filename argv element, no shell command construction; it owns the direct child PID and output pipe. Only **the reader fd** is nonblocking, while the FFmpeg output fd remains blocking to preserve normal backpressure without EAGAIN/truncated PCM. `r2_audio_file_read` polls the PCM fd and a separately signalable `eventfd`; cancellation wakes a silent reader with `ECANCELED`. Incoming bytes are not arbitrarily capped, and the read only returns complete four-byte stereo PCM frames on the standard 48-kHz S16LE stream; malformed trailing samples fail rather than being silently dropped.
- **Ownership and shutdown:** decoder handle publication/cancellation is protected by Ears' transport mutex; an active reader never closes or frees its own decoder on EOF/error. Only its lifecycle owner may close after reader completion. Close cancels, closes the pipe, terminates the exact child with SIGTERM and escalates to SIGKILL if necessary, then reaps it; no shell PID can obscure the child. If the decoder exits unsuccessfully, EOF checking returns EIO instead of presenting successful decoding; reaped PIDs are invalidated before cleanup to avoid accidental signaling of reused process IDs. Ears persists the final source-closed journal entry even when read already marked the source inactive.
- **Regressions:** `tests/ears_file_cancel_runtime.c` runs the real Ears/file transport with a private deterministic `ffmpeg` test executable. It checks literal filename arguments containing quotes and shell metacharacters, consecutive complete PCM frames, normal EOF, nonzero child exit reported as EIO, cancellation of a child which outputs a frame then stalls, prompt reader join and decoder close, confirmed child PID reaping, and journaling of successful, failed, and canceled source closure. Build/launcher/CI and standalone SIGTERM source manifests include `AudioFile.c/.h`. Full Actions workflow **41390162021**, code commit `830ca6c6a340a2d1c92d6cfc809bb37dc7fa1130`, completed **SUCCESS** including strict production compilation and overnight SIGTERM.
- **Remaining boundaries:** this tests a real child and I/O pipe with synthetic FFmpeg output, **not** installed-host decoding of a real media file; host permissions/devices and user-verified hardware/database baseline are preserved. `SIGKILL` cannot force a kernel-uninterruptible child to exit instantly; OS-level uninterruptible process states are outside the software's guarantees. This transport supports one file-decoder reader and one lifecycle owner; callers must not free a decoder while a reader is active. Live Pulse audio already has a separate cancellable async transport. Multi-output live sink capture, other subsystem connections, and the complete repo-wide line-by-line audit remain open. Book/PDF visual reading through Eyes is still deferred.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-021 — Exact Ears source identity, microphone hot-route following, and Watch visual-session custody (CODE / FULL CI COMPLETE; HOST ACCEPTANCE OPEN)

- **Manual inspection performed this batch:** followed `AudioDiscovery.c/.h` native source, uncorked sink input and monitor selection; `Ears.c` explicit and default microphone/desktop open, decoder reopen, source name ownership and close; `AudioCapture.c` cancellation handshake, `AudioFile.c` decoder lifetime; `r2.c` microphone/desktop capture start, status, stop and Watch generation checks, Eyes open/close and continuous watcher, media open/stop and auxiliary display attention; `shell.c` source polling, camera/media/microphone/desktop start, reroute and cleanup; corresponding public `r2.h` interfaces, compiled C fixtures and Python source-level tests; and GitHub workflow commands. This is a **bounded manual inspection** of the named paths, not a claim of a complete whole-repository line-by-line inspection.
- **Source-name use-after-free:** `r2_ears_open_source(ears, ears->event.source_name)` used to close/free the event's name before passing that borrowed pointer into Pulse or the log; `r2_ears_open_file(ears, ears->event.source_name)` had the same danger when reopening FFmpeg from the current event. Both now make an exact-size owned identity/path copy before closing the old source, retain the original complete name, and release the owned copy on success or error. White-box synthetic Ears tests reopen a 850+ byte device identity using the actual borrowed event pointer; real FFmpeg child tests reopen a file from its event identity without losing argv/path and check final closure journaling.
- **Microphone route continuity:** Watch previously detected a changed monitor for desktop playback but not a changed microphone. Added `r2_ears_status_microphone_source` to snapshot the live event under the microphone lifecycle mutex. Watch now treats a confirmed different microphone name as a route change, stops **only its generation-owned** old mic capture and opens the exact newly discovered physical source. A failed discovery query never counts as proof of unplug or reroute. Explicitly opened/manual mic sessions are not owned or closed by stale Watch tokens. Runtime tests exercise exact name matching, route transition, manual takeover, and old-token refusal.
- **Visual ownership bug:** Watch previously stored plain `managed_camera`/`managed_media` flags and unconditionally called `r2_eyes_release_source` or `r2_vision_media_stop` when a known source vanished or Watch ended. A manual/other caller could replace that source while Watch retained the boolean, after which Watch would close the replacement or steal another media source. Introduced independent monotonic Watch generation claims for primary camera and auxiliary media display, protected by their respective lifecycle locks. Explicit opens/stops revoke old claims; Watch can now release only an exactly matching generation. Media Watch-open checks under the same lifecycle lock whether another caller started a display between status observation and open; if so it leaves the other caller's display unchanged rather than replacing it. Camera Watch-open refuses to adopt an already opened camera. Camera remains independent of the separate media receiver; raw capture still does not imply automatic semantic image analysis.
- **Tests and verification:** C runtime `tests/watch_visual_ownership_runtime.c` exercises actual core token helpers with synthetic available media and camera state: stale stop does not close replacement, manual stop revokes claim, matching media token releases its own generation, an already occupied media display cannot be overwritten by Watch, and an already open primary camera cannot be adopted by Watch. Updated existing Watch, visual producer, camera-media attention and dual-source regressions to assert these precise ownership semantics rather than obsolete unconditional closes. All original source manifests/builds retained. **Full GitHub Actions workflow 41393891758 passed** on code/test commit `30bc0045f2471ae5d39a7232f1dcaf9e6e2d94fa`, including strict production build, audio source/runtime tests, camera/media tests and overnight SIGTERM lifecycle.
- **Unresolved, preserved openly:** AudioDiscovery still selects a single active desktop playback sink/monitor and does **not** aggregate independent simultaneous sink routes; no decision to mix such independent streams or alter R2's attention behavior is assumed. Visual camera/media token tests use synthetic objects and are not a real V4L/FFmpeg/GUI device acceptance test; actual PipeWire hotplug, multiple host outputs and current hardware remain outside GitHub CI. The user's physical-host and five-DB/GPU checks already supplied remain accepted, not overwritten by synthetic CI. Full file-by-file audit, additional resource lifetimes and uninterrupted real-device runs still need work. Eyes PDF/book reading is still deferred. **Keep draft PR unmerged; no inference of zero remaining bugs.**


### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-022 — Concurrent independent playback outputs and explicitly chosen audio attention (CODE + COMPLETE CI; PHYSICAL HOST OPEN)

- **Manual inspection scope:** inspected `AudioDiscovery.c/.h` end-to-end: server/source/sink-input/sink callbacks, routing, native asynchronous discovery, failure handling, result ownership, and freeing; examined `Ears.c/.h` source-open/snapshot/close and transport capture contract, `Log.h` source logging, `AudioCapture.c` async connection/cancellation boundaries; traced `r2.c` physical mic/primary desktop workers, rolling history and per-source PCM notices, shutdown and Watch ownership, deliberate `EARS_LISTEN` parser and Whisper WAV handoff, autonomous-reflection sensor-action dispatch, system prompt, direct conversation source links, and tool-accumulation memory behavior; reviewed `shell.c` Watch discovery/reconciliation/teardown and explicit desktop stop; read and extended the relevant compiled C tests, Python action/prompt/build smoke tests and GitHub workflow. This is an explicitly scoped manual audit, not the completed whole-repository line-by-line audit.
- **Root discovery gap:** the native sink-input callback previously stopped at the first uncorked playback stream, so simultaneous physical output sinks were hidden before attention could be chosen. `R2AudioDiscovery` now retains dynamically allocated `playback_outputs` for **every distinct active sink index** (one capture per sink, not per application), preserving each complete monitor name and representative actual PID. Callback deduplicates applications sharing a sink. All monitor names are resolved by real PA sink introspection; an inconsistent/stale sink index or failed native query fails the snapshot without claiming a device unplugged. The historical first-active `desktop_monitor` and default-only manual-listening fallback remain backward compatible, and nested strings/arrays are freed.
- **Independent capture paths:** `r2.c` now reconciles Watch-owned supplementary desktop outputs using actual monitor names and independent `R2Ears` receivers, worker threads, 16-second individual PCM rings, source-specific low-level amplitude-change notices, and isolated source teardown. No hardcoded source count limit or name truncation was introduced: buffers are allocated to the number of discovered sinks. The primary desktop stream and physical microphone are not displaced or intermixed. A failed discovery snapshot preserves existing captures. Confirmed source removal, output migration or deliberate desktop close releases only affected supplementary streams; Watch shutdown joins all supplementary readers.
- **Manual-primary coverage bug:** skipping the first discovered monitor even if the primary listener had been opened manually on **another** sink could leave the first active output uncaptured. Watch now snapshots the **actual current primary desktop monitor identity** under the capture lock and excludes only that selected source. When no primary receiver is currently active, all discovered output monitors are eligible for supplementary capture.
- **Deliberate R2 attention:** `[EARS_SOURCES]` reports actually active output monitor identities on demand, including the primary, with no implicit ASR or preference change; the returned list uses owned dynamically sized output, not a 512-byte or 8192-byte clipping buffer. `[EARS_LISTEN] desktop_source_recent|exact-monitor|seconds` and `desktop_source_live|exact-monitor|seconds` allow choosing an independent output explicitly (1–15 seconds). The exact unshortened source identity is retained in the listening result and PCM from other outputs/mic is not mixed. Future PCM requests are tied to a stream generation so they cannot silently splice a replaced route. If the chosen source is absent, ended, changed or insufficiently buffered, audio inspection fails; no audio meaning is fabricated. The ordinary mic/primary-desktop listening modes remain. The same read-only source discovery is now handled by **autonomous reflection**, not merely advertised in its prompt; a final reflection cannot execute another tool recursively.
- **Result allocation correctness:** `process_tools_with_status` previously used an 8192-byte local `snprintf` buffer and inserted an explicit truncation marker for longer results. It now allocates the exact formatted action result with `asprintf`, then grows its result buffer with overflow guards. Critically, it retains the existing authoritative fail-closed OOM message and original-size fallback used by the conversation recovery logic rather than risking replay of a possibly completed action. No project instructions were shortened to fit an arbitrary buffer.
- **Regression evidence:** native synthetic Pulse discovery exercises two separate active sink IDs, one duplicated app on the same sink, long distinct monitor names (>850 chars), verified default first-active compatibility, and stale-index failure. New compiled `tests/ears_multi_output_runtime.c` runs **12 independent output names**, captures per-source raw PCM, tests recent and future explicit audio selection, verifies >8192-character full output identity listing through the actual tool accumulator, exercises reroute and manually selected alternate primary, stops absent sources, and rejoins streams safely. Existing `ears_attention_tool_smoke.py`, `r2_sound_wiring_smoke.py` and `conversation_recovery_smoke.py` confirm the new explicit behavior without regressing model/command semantics or the fail-closed OOM warning.
- **Validated:** complete GitHub workflow run **41405718900** on code/test commit **`cf3164b5a3f5238cd0d65bedf4a1bd0f54b11726`** completed **SUCCESS**, including strict production compile, all new audio cases, previous Ears/Watch visual ownership and independent audio checks, existing memory/reality/action regressions, and isolated overnight SIGTERM integration. Prior superseded mid-edit CI failures were corrected rather than disregarded; this entry names only the final passing version.
- **Still open / not silently assumed:** physical target-host multi-sink PipeWire/Pulse routing, rapid hot-plug, simultaneous real camera/movie/mic use and service restart have **not** been witnessed by GitHub CI; hardware and database facts already verified separately by the user remain accepted as provided. PCM level changes are **not** a general non-speech sound classifier, and R2 decides whether to investigate. New Watch-managed extra-output connects remain bounded by the underlying Pulse connection handshake rather than guaranteed immediate cancellation during an in-progress open; inspect and improve this stop boundary next. Other unfinished modules and full whole-repository manual audit remain open. Deferred PDF/book visual-reading feature remains deferred. PR #179 stays draft and unmerged; success does not establish zero remaining bugs.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-023 — Multi-output review and complete-frame decoder contract (SCOPED INSPECTION; CODE CI SUCCESS)

- **Manual scope reviewed:** `AudioDiscovery.c/.h` distinct active sink introspection and source ownership, `shell.c` Watch reconciliation vs physical microphone and primary/media streams, `r2.c` supplemental per-sink receiver lifecycle, generation-safe rolling history, explicit per-monitor copy and recent/live PCM reader, `tests/ears_multi_output_runtime.c` synthetic 12-monitor capture and exact identities, `AudioFile.c` decoder poll/read framing and cancellation, `tests/ears_file_cancel_runtime.c` executable fake FFmpeg end-to-end read/cancel/error test, and CI manifest. This is **not** the whole-repository audit.
- **Confirmed existing multi-output implementation** from prior scoped entry 022: separate sink monitor identities are discovered, independently captured and buffered, while microphone and primary desktop input remain independent. Multiple applications sharing one sink still share its actual Pulse monitor; sources on different sinks are never blindly PCM-mixed. No automatic sound classification, transcription, attention change or invented meaning was introduced.
- **Corrected a PCM decoder contract violation:** `r2_audio_file_read` previously accepted any positive caller buffer size and returned `copied == bytes` even when that was 1–3 bytes of a 4-byte 48kHz stereo S16LE frame. Now invalid non-frame-aligned requests fail immediately with `EINVAL` **before consuming any FFmpeg audio**, and the success path only returns complete stereo frames. A real decoder-child regression passes a three-byte request, checks `EINVAL`, and confirms that the following ordinary four-byte read still yields the original first sample frame.
- **Validation:** GitHub Actions run **41432261903**, code/test head `7ec55f61929fdd72eb3fdb51dff028672a354d1f` completed **SUCCESS** with full configured suite, including the independent 12-output capture test, decoder cancellation and framing test, strict production build and overnight SIGTERM smoke. No target host or physical multichannel acceptance is claimed.
- **Known boundaries unchanged:** current supplemental-monitor open holds the reconciliation lock during the bounded Pulse connect handshake; rapid shutdown of a just-starting extra monitor has a latency boundary to examine. Physical PipeWire multi-output route changes and model attention selection under real environmental sound remain unverified. Deferred book/PDF visual reading stays deferred. PR #179 remains draft/unmerged; additional files and cross-module paths still require manual inspection.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-024 — Cancel supplementary-output connection setup before lifecycle joins (LOCAL VALIDATION; GITHUB CI PENDING)

- **Manual inspection scope:** traced `r2.c` supplementary-output reconciliation, receiver allocation/publication, cancellation, worker startup, ring-history readers, source removal, explicit desktop stop, Watch stop, shutdown request and teardown; `shell.c` coordinator stop predicate, reconciliation call and join ordering; `Ears.c/.h` live-source reset, transport publication, connect failure, cancellation and close ownership; every line of `AudioCapture.c/.h` including event-loop startup, timed handshake, callbacks, read/wake, cancellation and resource disposal; relevant source manifests and existing/new compiled audio regressions. This is a scoped continuation, not the completed repository-wide every-line inspection.
- **Root lifecycle issue:** supplementary reconciliation held `extra_desktop_lock` throughout each Pulse handshake. `r2_ears_extra_stop_all` needed that same lock before it could reach/cancel the connecting receiver, so stop could wait for the handshake timeout and subsequent outputs could start during the same reconciliation. A separate pending-receiver lifetime mutex now lets stop signal the receiver without acquiring the reconciliation mutex. The lifecycle owner clears that protected pointer before releasing the receiver; cancellation never assumes ownership of free/join. A stop epoch invalidates an older reconciliation and an active-stopper count protects concurrent stop callers. Cancelled reconciliation does not continue through later discovered outputs.
- **Pre-open/reset race:** publishing the receiver alone was insufficient: Ears resets its reusable cancellation flag when opening a source. Added a cancelable exact-monitor open whose owner's nonblocking stop predicate is checked after reset and after transport publication. Watch passes its persistent coordinator stop predicate through reconciliation, preventing a stop immediately preceding reconciliation from being forgotten. Watch stop and core shutdown request signal pending opens before a coordinator/lifecycle join. Existing noncancelable public APIs retain their behavior; physical mic, primary desktop and independent PCM buffers/identities remain separate.
- **Transport concurrency:** `AudioCapture.c` previously read/wrote the plain `mainloop_started` field concurrently during connect/cancel. It is now an initialized atomic. A capture cancelled before event-loop startup reports `ECANCELED`, rather than misreporting a valid cancelled request as `EINVAL`.
- **Regression coverage:** `tests/ears_extra_connect_cancel_runtime.c` runs the actual core and Ears with a deliberately blocked transport handshake. It covers stop during connect, stop before Ears resets state, Watch stop before join, owner cancellation preceding reconciliation, core shutdown request, exact cleanup, and refusal to open the next two outputs; stopped scenarios must join within one second. Existing 12-output regression still verifies separate PCM, full >850-character identities, >8192-character source lists, explicit recent/live selection, reroutes, manual alternate primary and teardown. New `tests/ears_connect_cancel_runtime.c` additionally uses actual libpulse against a silent local Unix socket to test a stalled protocol handshake and pre-start cancellation without a physical audio server; this test requires a socket-capable execution environment.
- **Validation boundary:** strict production build passed; all 13 selected audio/Watch workflow checks passed locally, including core/Ears cancellation, 12-output PCM isolation, FFmpeg cancellation/framing, native discovery, Whisper conversion, audio/visual ownership and continuous Eyes wiring. Workflow YAML and every run-step shell script also parse successfully. The silent-socket libpulse test cannot execute in the current workspace because `socket(AF_UNIX, SOCK_STREAM, 0)` is denied. Both new tests are wired into the existing full GitHub workflow. Automatic approval review rejected pushing this workload to the existing audit branch; the changes remain local pending explicit push approval. No new full GitHub CI success or physical-host result is claimed.
- **Still open:** supplementary connection setup still serializes normal list/history queries behind the reconciliation mutex; this change specifically makes stop notification independent of that mutex. Cancellation depends on a schedulable Pulse event loop and is not a guarantee against arbitrary kernel stalls. Target-host simultaneous camera/movie/mic and multi-output routing acceptance, remaining connected subsystems, full allocation review and whole-repository manual inspection remain unfinished. Previously user-verified DB/GPU baselines remain accepted. PDF/book visual reading remains deferred. PR #179 remains draft and must not be merged by this workload.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-025 — Primary input cancellation, generation-safe live attention and complete transcripts (CODE; VALIDATION IN PROGRESS)

- **Inspected execution paths:** continued the manual trace through `shell.c` native discovery, Watch microphone/desktop starts, ownership and coordinator joins; `r2.c` primary audio opens/stops, direct one-shot capture, complete source-event snapshots, both ring buffers and live/recent attention requests, local Whisper child/output handling, shutdown request and shutdown ordering; `Ears.c/.h` default/exact source resolution and open/reset/cancel/error cleanup; `AudioDiscovery.c/.h` native connection pump, metadata pump and cancellation/failure cleanup. Read the affected runtime regressions and workflow commands. This does not mark unrelated files or the whole repository as fully inspected.
- **Primary-open stop gap:** microphone/primary desktop setup still held lifecycle mutexes through the handshake, while Watch stop only notified supplementary outputs. Added independently protected pending primary receiver references, per-input stop epochs, active-stop counts and Watch-only cancellation epochs. Exact physical microphone and primary monitor opens now receive durable cancellation predicates across Ears reset. Watch passes its own persistent stop predicate into start calls, so late starts after coordinator stop fail instead of creating new captures. Watch stop cancels only its own pending opens; it cannot cancel a manual pending source. Explicit input stop cancels its own source; shutdown cancels pending inputs before worker joins. Pending references are removed before the lifecycle owner can close/free the receiver.
- **Direct-listen shutdown gap:** an already-connected one-shot microphone read had ceased to be registered as pending, so shutdown could join a caller still blocked waiting for PCM. Direct capture now retains a protected, non-Watch receiver reference for the complete read interval. Both shutdown request and direct shutdown signal these inputs before joins. No shutdown caller assumes transport free/join ownership.
- **Discovery cancellation:** added a cancelable native-discovery entry point while preserving the existing noncancelable API. Connection and metadata pumps poll the owner predicate, return `ECANCELED`, and clean up without interpreting failure as source disappearance. Default source opens and Watch use this route. Existing bounded deadline remains for a server that fails independently of stop. The shared core Watch-running flag is now atomic rather than a plain cross-thread integer.
- **Live PCM generation bug:** primary microphone/desktop future-interval readers previously relied only on a byte counter that resets on source replacement. A replacement could produce enough PCM before a waiting reader resumed and be accepted as the requested original interval. Both primary histories now carry generation counters, updated under their history mutex on start/reset/stop. Future requests snapshot generation and start offset together, then reject replacement PCM before copying. No samples from different generations are spliced. Existing supplementary generation checks remain. All four primary buffer-copy helpers reject non-frame-aligned lengths.
- **Exact primary journal identity:** continuous primary workers previously emitted level-change and stream-ended events under generic `microphone`, `desktop_playback`, `Ears` or `Ears desktop` source labels. Each worker now holds an owned snapshot of its actual complete source identity for its generation and uses that identity in both event paths; release happens on worker exit, before the lifecycle owner releases the source.
- **Transcript allocation:** completed local recognizer output was previously rejected above a fixed 16,384-byte read limit. The reader now grows with the complete output via `getdelim`, still rejecting embedded NUL, read errors, close errors and empty results. It does not silently shorten transcript text. Runtime regression invokes a local fake recognizer and verifies all 40,000 expected characters survive the real capture/normalization/child/output path.
- **Regression scope:** expanded actual core/Ears stalled-transport tests to cover microphone and primary desktop, before/after Ears reset, explicit stop, Watch stop, shutdown request, manual pending-open custody and a truly blocked direct PCM read. Added primary-history runtime tests that replace a source while its live request waits, require `ECANCELED` with zero substituted bytes, verify valid-generation PCM, and preserve >850-character actual source names through level-change and capture-ended events. Native discovery regression cancels a stalled query pump and checks a cancelled result is not reported as a successful empty snapshot. Existing 12 independent output, ownership, decoder and visual regressions are retained and interface assertions updated.
- **Branch and publication:** the user explicitly approved uploading all relevant code/tests and requested a new branch. This workload is on `audit/r2-audio-continuity-cancellation-2026-11-02`, based on the existing audit head plus local entry-024 fixes. The earlier push-approval limitation recorded in entry 024 is superseded by that explicit approval. Keep the continuation draft; do not merge either draft.
- **Verification boundaries:** the current workspace can run strict production compilation, synthetic/native-callback regressions and the local fake-recognizer child path; it denies Unix socket creation, so the actual libpulse silent-server handshake regression requires GitHub CI. No physical PipeWire microphone/movie/camera run, hardware model inference or installed database revalidation is claimed. Full repository-wide manual inspection and remaining allocation/resource paths continue; PDF/book visual reading stays deferred. Final CI evidence is recorded separately after observing the exact pushed head.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-026 — Desktop attention still depended on the microphone lifecycle (LOCAL REGRESSION PASSED; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Inspected connection:** followed the complete `r2_ears_listen_cancelable` source-selection branches after entry 025, including lock acquisition/release, stop predicates, ring snapshots, recognizer invocation and transcript return.
- **Finding:** both primary desktop live/recent attention branches unnecessarily acquired `ears_capture_lock` and rejected a request whenever a microphone stop requester existed. Consequently an unrelated direct microphone capture or pending microphone stop could block/reject a valid independent desktop listening action. Supplementary-source attention also inherited the initial microphone stop check.
- **Repair:** desktop requests use their own input stop predicate and lifecycle mutex; microphone locking/stop checks now belong only to the microphone branch. Supplementary requests retain their independent PCM selection and source-generation checks. No cross-source mixing, forced interruption or semantic interpretation was added.
- **Runtime evidence:** expanded `tests/ears_watch_ownership_runtime.c` configures a real local fake recognizer returning 40,000 expected characters, seeds a valid primary-desktop recent interval, holds the microphone lifecycle mutex and announces an active microphone stop concurrently, then invokes desktop attention on another thread. Desktop recognition must finish within the bounded test wait and return the complete expected transcript while the microphone mutex remains held. The regression passes locally alongside the earlier takeover/one-shot-stop tests.
- **Publication boundary:** draft continuation PR #180 exists on the newly requested branch. Prior code head `b82153c8abf6ffab80e2ff7d9feb781a5befcd38` already passed the actual-libpulse silent-local-server handshake test and all new audio steps in workflow 41466271154; that run is not evidence for this later change. Record the final updated head and whole-workflow outcome separately. Full repository-wide manual inspection, physical-host acceptance and deferred PDF/book reading status remain unchanged.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-027 — Exact selected-audio provenance through ASR and conversation (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Manual scope:** traced `r2.c` explicit EARS_LISTEN parsing and action-result allocation, primary/supplementary recent/live PCM selection, generation checks, direct microphone capture, temporary WAV normalization, recognizer spawn/wait/output/cleanup, public conversation serialization, final turn logging and source associations; `r2.h` ownership contracts; `shell.c` speech entry and common conversation path; affected runtime tests and manifests. These are scoped paths, not a claim that every line of the full source files was inspected.
- **Provenance loss:** primary microphone/desktop tool results used generic source labels, and `speech_transcribed` always claimed a microphone under the generic source `r2_ears_listen`. A device change during recognition could no longer be related to the exact captured interval; shell speech's direct microphone was already closed before normal conversation source snapshots occurred. Added a source-aware listening API that snapshots primary identities under their capture lifecycle mutexes and validates named supplementary identity against generation-checked PCM. It keeps the owned identity through recognition, records the complete transcript under that actual source, and optionally returns the owned source plus exact durable speech-event ID. Existing text-only public listening APIs remain wrappers. Failed recognition returns no source/event output and creates no interpreted event.
- **Real-data relationship:** shell speech still uses the existing serialized shell/core conversation implementation. It supplies the original speech-event ID through an optional metadata wrapper; typed calls use no such ID. Explicit EARS_LISTEN results inside a turn collect their own exact speech-event IDs in a dynamically allocated, thread-local turn scope. After the conversation event is persisted, exact ID relationships connect it to the actual recognized speech events. This does not infer relationships from duplicated transcript text or whichever device happens to be open afterward. Allocation/link failures are reported; already executed listening is not replayed. Autonomous tool use outside a conversation still records its standalone exact sensory event without inventing a user turn.
- **Named-primary gap:** EARS_SOURCES listed the primary desktop monitor, but the named-monitor EARS_LISTEN route only searched supplementary captures. Exact named selection now recognizes the primary as well, rechecks its identity under the actual capture lock and retains generation checks. Route changes between selection and capture fail instead of silently substituting another source.
- **Runtime coverage:** `tests/audio_provenance_persistence_runtime.c` uses actual core selection, WAV normalization, an executable local fake recognizer, Life Log, diary, Sensory Journal and SQLite. It changes the device's reported identity while recognition completes and requires the returned/persisted original >850-character identity, including an internal pipe; exercises microphone, desktop and explicitly named primary selection, full tool results and exact event links; closes every test database connection, shuts the subsystems down and reopens them before checking durable source/text/link identities. Embedded-NUL recognizer output must fail with NULL/0 outputs and no new sensory entry. Real hardware/recognition accuracy is not inferred from this fixture.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-028 — Durable conversation cuts and recall-allocation limits (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Manual scope:** inspected `Log.c` event validation and SQLite binding, complete conversation event/archive writer, query-result allocation, main/linked event formatting and query cleanup; `SensoryJournal.c` literal search-pattern construction, SQLite event projection, complete result serialization and error/overflow cleanup; followed the shared database/index calls used by the source-aware speech path. Existing schema and previously verified installed DB/GPU baselines remain unchanged.
- **Silent durable loss:** `r2_log_conversation_turn` reduced each user/assistant payload to approximately 32 KiB before both its chronological event and dedicated conversation-table insert. It now allocates the exact complete combined details with overflow checks and binds full user/assistant text using SQLite's length-aware 64-bit text API. Concise summaries remain previews; authoritative payload columns are complete. The generic event writer no longer rejects valid complete details merely for exceeding 64 KiB; SQLite's real limits/allocation errors remain explicit. If the dedicated archive insert fails, the full event text still preserves the turn, and the failure is reported. Previously clipped historical records are not fabricated or reconstructed.
- **Recall-side loss/failure:** Life Log's append helper imposed a 64 KiB result ceiling and shortened linked event notes/summary/details. The helper now grows to the complete selected records with overflow checks, and linked records retain their actual text. Sensory Journal similarly grows beyond its former 2 MiB truncation boundary, reporting allocation/serialization failure rather than returning an intentionally incomplete prefix. Requested row limits remain separate from complete-record serialization.
- **False long-source matches:** Sensory Journal silently used only the first 1,024 bytes of a literal query. It now allocates/escapes the complete query; distinct source identities with a shared long prefix remain distinct. LIKE wildcard escaping is retained.
- **Regression evidence:** the combined fixture verifies exact 100,000/130,000-character-scale user/assistant payloads in both storage forms, complete linked speech recall, retained full event details when a trigger rejects the dedicated archive insert, retrieval of a 2.2-million-character record through both Life Log and Sensory Journal, and two >1,600-character source queries that differ only beyond the old cut (including literal `%`, `_`, `!`). No production databases are accessed by these tests.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-02-029 — Whole-file reads could accept partial historical context (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Manual scope:** traced all `read_entire_file` call sites: regular workspace text reading and archived startup conversation loading; file-open/stat/read/error/close handling, archive prompt allocation and pinned-message retention.
- **Finding:** the old reader measured an initial file size, allocated that size and returned whatever shorter byte count fread produced without checking read failure or an incomplete snapshot. Embedded NUL also made a valid-looking prefix masquerade as complete text. Startup ignored failed archive reads and did not inspect the pinned-message insertion result.
- **Repair:** regular text is read into a growing allocation through EOF; reads shorter than the initial regular-file size, embedded NUL, read/stat/close errors and allocation failures are rejected. Empty text remains valid. A file growing during the read is not clipped to its initial size. Missing optional startup archive remains supported; an existing archive that cannot be read completely is an explicit initialization failure. Archive formatting uses exact dynamic allocation and failure to retain the full pinned message also fails initialization through the existing cleanup owner.
- **Fault coverage:** the new combined runtime fixture injects a short successful-looking read and an I/O failure and requires rejection, appends data after initial stat and requires the whole grown text, rejects embedded NUL, and accepts an empty regular file. Existing archive/current-turn and startup cleanup regressions remain required.
- **Documentation:** corrected `docs/local-speech-input.md` to match the implemented independent source/Watch ownership behavior and exact speech-event path; removed obsolete statements that Watch refuses existing sources, blocks standalone Ears commands or never opens an available camera.
- **Still open:** physical target-host simultaneous input/recognition acceptance and the whole-repository manual/source/allocation audit remain unfinished. This workload does not add deferred PDF/book visual reading or assign automatic meaning/attention to sound. Continue draft PR #180 without merging. The final exact CI head/outcome is recorded after publication and completion.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-04-030 — Structured recall boundaries and complete inference units (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Manual scope:** traced Life Log query formatting and linked-event expansion, overlapping keyword search, record deduplication, legacy excerpt assembly, final message selection, JSON serialization and configured inference allocation.
- **Finding:** chat treated blank lines in formatted event text as record boundaries and parsed bracketed numbers as identities. Ordinary paragraphs split one actual event; quoted event headers could suppress a different event. Per-search character cuts and later aggregate caps also lost pieces of selected Life Log records.
- **Repair:** the query writer now optionally returns durable event IDs with exact serialized byte spans. Chat deduplicates those actual IDs, validates the spans, grows its allocations for full selected records and appends these records after legacy excerpt caps. Existing human-readable query APIs remain available. Requested row/search limits remain selection policy; they no longer shorten the records selected through this path.
- **Inference repair:** provisional category budgets previously shortened selected evidence and instructions again during serialization. A final whole-message allocation reserves complete core/current instructions, selected current retrieval, actual user input and subsequent action handoff before optional old history. Complete selected messages are passed to JSON unchanged. An insufficient actual configured model allocation fails before transmission rather than sending prefixes; configured context/GPU settings are not enlarged without evidence of capacity. Staged inference for records larger than the actual model window remains open, as do older excerpt limits in other stores. Helper-generated system contracts without conversation section headings are required too; only explicitly identified historical/startup/learning snapshots may remain optional. Runtime coverage also verifies that a helper request cannot proceed with its instructions dropped when only the user text fits.
- **Regression evidence:** the new real-SQLite runtime fixture retrieves a >24,000-byte event containing paragraphs and a forged quoted ID alongside the actual second event; both correct records survive, repeated searches deduplicate only actual IDs, and JSON message content is compared byte-for-byte. It requires whole optional-history omission and verifies insufficient capacity leaves selection masks/source data untouched. Source-level checks also trace the final allocator into the actual serializer.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-04-031 — Exact speech/reflection/diary provenance (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Manual scope:** followed autonomous EARS_LISTEN dispatch, thread-local evidence ownership, follow-up generation, user-activity discard/shutdown cleanup, diary insertion and completion-event persistence; inspected Sensory Journal's legacy duplicate-text ambiguity checks.
- **Finding:** deliberate listening during autonomous reflection stored standalone speech but did not link that exact evidence to the final reflection. Reflection-to-diary association still relied on matching prose, which becomes ambiguous when two real entries have identical text.
- **Repair:** diary writes optionally return their actual inserted SQLite row ID while the existing wrapper stays compatible. Reflection persistence uses that row ID plus its actual completion-event ID, stored in an additive dedicated association table. Sensory Journal prefers this exact association and retains existing ambiguity protection for legacy guessed links. The autonomous cycle collects only speech events from its actual listening dispatch, restores the prior thread-local owner immediately and frees evidence on every post-generation exit. Exact speech IDs link to the completed reflection. Manual reflection shares the same diary/completion persistence path. Secondary link failures are reported without pretending the authoritative diary insert failed or replaying actions.
- **Regression evidence:** two reflections with identical prose and different actual speech-event IDs must retain distinct diary IDs and correct speech links after all connections/subsystems close and reopen. Sensory Journal displays both exact diary links despite duplicate prose; linking a non-reflection event through the new API is rejected. No attention policy, sensory mixing or automatic interpretation was added.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-04-038 — Finished HTTP responses could contain unfinished or prefix-only text (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Manual scope:** response callback accumulation, JSON lifetime, role/done/final-channel projection, generation metadata, final reply allocation, error event persistence, conversation retries and the later control-marker dispatch boundary.
- **Findings/repairs:** `done=true` with explicit `done_reason=length` was accepted as a complete outward reply, even when generation stopped mid-answer or mid-action request. The primary Ollama implementation distinguishes this stop reason: https://github.com/ollama/ollama/blob/main/llm/llama_server.go and https://github.com/ollama/ollama/blob/main/llm/server.go. Such final-channel output is now archived as explicitly incomplete error evidence and rejected before callers can treat it as a reply/action. No automatic action replay is introduced. Existing conversation retries/fallback remain owned by their original caller. Private thinking is never archived through this path.
- **Related text-loss repair:** JSON final-channel strings containing embedded NUL were copied with C-string length and accepted as prefixes. The response now compares the actual JSON string length; invalid text is rejected and its complete escaped JSON-string representation is retained as error evidence, without private reasoning. Archival failures are reported. Existing role/done checks remain; legacy responses omitting done_reason still use the established completion contract.
- **Runtime evidence:** a 70,000-byte-scale incomplete final channel beginning with an action marker must return NULL, retain the full partial output in the real Life Log, exclude private thinking and survive database close/reopen. The actual response callback receives split chunks before parsing. Separate cases reject done=false and embedded-NUL text, retain both sides of the NUL in escaped evidence, and accept a legacy completed response without done_reason. This proves transport/parser/store boundaries, not actual host tokenization, generation quality or complete action-system acceptance.
- **Verification/next work:** local strict production build, the expanded runtime fixture and related source checks passed. Exact final CI evidence follows publication. ### R2-AUD-2026-11-04-041 — Owned JPEG files, complete reads and bounded encoder-child cancellation (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

- **Manual scope:** RGB shape validation, PPM/JPEG creation, all pre-fork exits, child launch/wait, temporary-file ownership, read/close handling and raw-frame base64 caller. Checked the existing shared cancellation predicate and source-worker caller context; camera/media acquisition and attention decisions are unchanged.
- **Findings/repairs:** a PPM write/close failure removed only the PPM and leaked its already-created JPEG. The encoder waited indefinitely for its exact FFmpeg child. JPEG reading consumed only the initial stat size, accepting a prefix if a file grew, and ignored close failure. Both owned files now clean up on early write/close failure. The owned child is polled at 25 ms, observes existing shutdown/foreground cancellation and has an explicit 30-second encoder deadline; cancellation/timeout kills and reaps only that child before artifact cleanup. This is an encoder resource deadline, not a data-prefix or attention policy. Reads grow through EOF within the existing explicit 20 MiB image limit, reject short/error/oversized reads and require successful close. Original frame/image size validation remains.
- **Runtime evidence:** actual file I/O plus synthetic fork/wait/kill/time boundaries verify PPM write/close failures leave neither owned temporary file, cancellation and timeout signal only the owned PID and reap it once, and all encoder artifacts disappear. A file growing after stat must return every byte; short/error reads and failed close return NULL/zero length. The child/time test does not run real FFmpeg or prove target-host scheduling/throughput. Real OS/device shutdown and overnight acceptance remain separate.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-04-042 — In-flight analysis could write into a replacement visual library (CODE; FINAL CI PENDING)

The old request would then insert into that replacement using its earlier snapshot. A monotonic lock-protected library generation is captured with the model/path. Generations are never reused on overflow. Locks are not held across encoding/HTTP.

The stale result returns NULL, the replacement remains empty, and the original library's valid row survives reopen with its exact event link. The normal legacy-schema write test passes, and all 48 selected related source guards pass locally. Strict production compilation and the expanded runtime fixture pass locally; final full CI evidence follows publication.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-05-043 — Commit Visual overlays in request order

- Traced description parsing, JPEG archive creation, SQLite insertion, session shutdown and the separate Eyes status cleanup. The parser previously published focus before either storage operation succeeded. Failed requests could also clear an unrelated overlay, and completion order could replace a newer result with an older one.
- Keep an owned focus reference until storage succeeds. Under the Visual library lock, publish only from the current generation and only when the request is newer than the last committed overlay decision. A successful result without a focus clears the overlay. Failed inference, archive creation and inserts preserve the last committed overlay. Shutdown clears it under the same lock. Use a unique temporary file and atomic rename for publication.
- The production-path fixture starts a second analysis during the first request's archive write, verifies the newer focus after both complete, then forces an archive collision, incomplete inference and SQLite insert rejection. It checks that the overlay survives those failures and clears on shutdown. The Visual runtime and three related source checks passed locally. Full CI follows publication.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-05-044 — Bind V-Webcam focus to the capture session

- Traced Eyes source open, capture, EOF and close, the primary and auxiliary frame-copy paths in `r2.c`, Visual's archive and SQLite commit, and V-Webcam's refresh, pause and overlay rendering. Auxiliary frames have no preview token. Visual saves valid observations as before and updates the display only for frames from the active preview session. The viewer verifies the token on the open frame descriptor and only draws matching focus. Pausing holds the visible frame and focus together. Full focus labels reach the viewer.
- The independent Eyes runtime exercised same-name reopen and auxiliary close. The Visual fixture switched preview sessions during synthetic HTTP and verified that both the old camera and auxiliary observations remained in the real archive/database without changing preview focus. The viewer regression exercised the actual refresh and file readers with a stub image decoder, including mismatched or missing files and paused refresh. Legacy Visual database writes also passed. Full CI follows publication.
- This scope covers session provenance in the display. Focus remains a region reported for the session as its frames advance. ### R2-AUD-2026-11-08-057 — Cancel stalled visual frame reads before joining workers

- Manually traced `Eyes.c` decoder launch/read/EOF/close, `r2.c` camera/media capture, first-frame waiters, stop/join, direct foreground captures and camera reopening. Both workers held capture mutexes during blocking `fread`; stop cleared an unrelated atomic then joined before closing the stream. A stalled decoder could therefore prevent shutdown indefinitely. A new runtime fixture timed out after five seconds on the original code.
- Start capture FFmpeg directly with argument arrays and a private process group. Keep the reader nonblocking and the writer blocking. Frame assembly polls the caller's stop/shutdown predicate without discarding partial data or treating it as a frame. Stop closes a cancelled partial stream. Cleanup closes the pipe, sends TERM, escalates after 250 ms if necessary, and reaps the owned child. Nonzero decoder exit and incomplete-frame EOF are errors, not clean EOF. Existing capture callers retain the original wrapper API.
- Camera/media continuous workers and direct core captures now supply cancellation predicates. Camera reopening stops/reaps the previous worker before replacing its source or reusing latest-frame state. Source-open availability is atomic; other frame/source ownership contracts remain unchanged. No inference or attention decision was added to raw capture.
- Strict production compilation, camera/media wiring and independent-frame fixtures passed locally. The new real-process fixture passed with synthetic FFmpeg pixels: a decoder stalled before frame one, another stalled mid-frame, both ignoring TERM, bounded worker stop, exact child reaping, restart with byte-exact complete frames, incomplete EOF rejection and nonzero decoder-exit rejection. The five-second baseline timeout establishes the old hang; it is not hardware acceptance. Full CI follows publication.
- This completes the scoped blocking-frame trace, not all Eyes inspection. Metadata probes/X11 discovery still use separate subprocess paths requiring cancellation review. Physical camera/X11 capture, actual model behavior and overnight operation remain unverified here. Audit commit is the commit introducing this entry; final verification records its SHA.

Added an exact worker token under the existing lifecycle mutex. Manual start adopts an existing worker and revokes discovery's claim; a discovery start cannot claim an already-running worker. Cleanup stops only the matching generation. Existing public start/stop APIs retain their behavior.
- Follow-up compatibility inspection of audit 057 distinguished worker cancellation from closing the camera source. Closing a cancelled partial stream also closed a manually opened camera when Watch only owned its worker. Cancellation now preserves the open source and its exact partial frame offset. A later reader resumes that frame without shifted RGB bytes. Explicit source release/media stop still closes, terminates and reaps the decoder after its reader exits. This supersedes audit 057's cancellation-closes-source implementation; genuine EOF/read errors still close it.
- The real-process lifecycle fixture passed stale-token cleanup after manual adoption, refusal to claim a running worker, independent replacement generations, matching-token stop, camera-source survival after worker stop, explicit source release/reaping, and byte-exact continuation after cancellation five bytes into a twelve-byte RGB frame. ### R2-AUD-2026-11-08-068 — Carry exact renderer identity through Watch and FFmpeg

- Read Watch's complete media decision, open, stop, shutdown and token handling in `shell.c` and `r2.c`. Traced X11 subprocess search, candidate geometry, title provenance, RGB frame sizing and decoder cleanup in `Eyes.c`. Watch's VLC fallback discarded the discovered window ID, then searched again at open. Consequently, changing VLC windows could retain a stale receiver or capture a different window from the one discovered. The CRT could also withdraw its publication while an unrelated PulseAudio failure kept the old renderer alive.
- Preserve the selected X11 ID through Watch's comparison and the exact VLC decoder open, retaining VLC title metadata and existing public entry points. Choose the largest valid visible candidate for VLC and producer searches, reject signed and out-of-range XIDs, and pass probed dimensions to FFmpeg so capture and the raw frame assembler agree. Keep exact ownership and CRT priority, honor explicit CRT withdrawal independently of audio discovery, and recheck a publication around decoder startup.
- The compiled Watch reconciliation fixture verified VLC replacement, steady-state reuse, CRT withdrawal, a race during open, manual takeover, and transient audio discovery loss. The subprocess fixture verified selection and rejection against synthetic X11 output. Ownership and raw capture fixtures cover decoder arguments, long source metadata and reaping. This completes the scoped X11 window transition; continued audit covers playback changes on a reused CRT XID and exact tape-event paths. The commit introducing this entry records the scope.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-08-069 — Separate visible CRT publication from VCR playback history

- Followed CRT state from `TV.py` refresh and libVLC's player state through the shell socket, Watch's priority/ownership logic, and the Life Log media event. The player can continue while the CRT is powered off or showing another input. Publishing solely from playback transitions could leave Watch on a hidden surface or fail to restore that surface when it became visible again. A reused Tk window could also carry attention into another tape, and legacy locator parsing could treat characters inside a filename as an XID.
- Publish the visible playback surface independently and withdraw it on hiding or unreadable display state. The shell snapshots XID and generation together, changes generation on exact tape identity changes, and accepts identical renewals without restarting capture. New exact-path events retain the whole filename and have no publication side effects. The legacy media-event command remains accepted. Reject embedded NUL in socket commands and out-of-range X11 locators.
- Native Watch and protocol fixtures verified same-window tape replacement, idempotent renewal, withdrawal, legacy playback messages and exact paths containing the old delimiter. Python fixtures exercised hide/restore, event deduplication and rejected inputs. This completes the scoped publisher/Watch trace; the next entry covers saved tape position identity. The commit introducing this entry records the scope.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-08-070 — Save position for the actual tape and commit insertion atomically

- Read `TV.py` save/replace/pause/close, shell position parsing, Reality's tape and cassette writes, and the migrated SQLite fixture. The old GUI saved only a timestamp, so a write arriving after Reality selected a different tape overwrote that new tape's position. Insertion also committed the history row before cassette selection and could report success when a trigger skipped selection.
- Send the outgoing tape's full path with its position and save before changing the loaded player or pausing. Reality writes the exact existing tape key. Keep the legacy active-tape command and API. Insert the history row and select the cassette in one transaction, requiring an updated authoritative row before committing.
- The native writable migration fixture saved the outgoing tape after switching, rejected a nonexistent tape, injected an ignored cassette update and verified rollback, reinserted the first tape with its saved position, and preserved the migrated cassette. The Python player fixture verified save-before-replacement with an exact delimiter-bearing path. The service fixture also exercises real socket parsing and SQLite verification in CI. This completes scoped position/write ownership. The commit introducing this entry records the scope.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-08-071 — Keep delayed VLC seeks with their exact load

- Followed `TV.py`'s asynchronous preparation, Reality snapshot, periodic transport sync, playback event reporting, and seek completion. A callback compared only the filename, so a superseded callback could apply when the same tape was loaded again. A transient SQLite read also let periodic sync resume the player before restoring the saved position.
- Scope each delayed seek to one load, retry a temporary Reality failure while the same load remains current, and hold transport and playback reporting until that load applies its saved position. Replacing or ejecting a tape invalidates its pending seek. This preserves the existing Reality authority and mute/seek/play order.
- Python regression exercised rapid A–B–A loading, ignored the superseded callback, injected a temporary SQLite error, held playback paused during the retry, and applied the latest saved position before resuming. The existing delayed-transport and position-save fixtures passed. This completes the scoped callback ownership trace. The commit introducing this entry records the scope.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-11-110 — Resume interrupted Markdown appends

- Traced diary insert through daily path selection, append, close, and restart. Every new SQLite entry commits a pending mirror record. A writer locks the daily file and persists its identity and original byte position before appending. SQLite uses FULL synchronization for those boundaries. Repair verifies existing bytes, appends the missing suffix, synchronizes the file, and acknowledges the operation while retaining the lock. A completed append with a pending acknowledgement is recognized without writing it again.
- A changed prefix or replaced file leaves the entry pending. Earlier pending entries block later writes to that day while other days can recover. The fixture covers a failed mirror, an interrupted append, a conflicting edit, and a fully written record with rejected acknowledgement, followed by restart and exact-byte verification. Historical partial appends without a reservation are outside automatic repair. External writers that bypass advisory locks still require operator coordination.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-11-112 — Fix two output integrity failures found in the full pass

Validate each field before formatting and reject the result if serialization cannot preserve it. A legacy SQLite fixture verifies the query fails with a binary string still intact in storage.
