### R2-AUD-2026-11-14-158 — Make failed history admission atomic and pin workspace directory reads

- Continued the manual production core read, including the live-history budget and eviction algorithm, directory path resolution, read/list/write/delete operations, task-output archival, model response paths, daemon startup and shutdown ownership, and sensor-source generation handoffs. The original single-message `message_add_ex` evicted non-pinned turns before proving an incoming message could coexist with pinned context; if eviction exhausted available history and admission still failed, existing live messages were already gone. It now plans removals, checks the whole byte/count budget and reserves array capacity before modifying any original entry. This preserves the bounded RAM design and the independent durable Life Log.
- The new native regression constructs an almost-full pinned message, one live human turn, and an additional message that cannot fit even after evicting the human turn. It verifies failure preserves the original message pointers, content, count and pinned state. This is distinct from the existing atomic two-message Hands result append. The bounded-message behavior does not imply an infinite model inference context.
- Workspace read previously reopened a mutable pathname after validation; directory listing had the same reopened-directory race. Both now descend through the pinned workspace directory with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and the file read consumes the resulting regular-file descriptor. A native fixture uses a real `openat` interception to swap a validated file and a validated subdirectory for symlinks pointing outside the workspace. Both requests must refuse the swap, while ordinary inside-workspace reads/listings must succeed.
- The exact production head `cc8bb516875c301eba9ca49900f6c81c81b5a939` passed all **123 of 123** steps in GitHub Actions run [43569555951](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/43569555951), including the new native fixtures, strict build, and isolated SIGTERM lifecycle integration. ### R2-AUD-2026-11-14-160 — Keep Game Boy facts and cartridge state together; pin diary workspace I/O

- Inspected the complete current Game Boy console and lifecycle fixture, service and isolated model runners, TV controller, Observer public log, V-Webcam display, TV installer, both systemd services, and the diary workspace read/list/search paths. These are additional current source reviews, not a retrospective claim that the older 173/184 whole-file inventory has been reconciled to the 186 tracked files in this branch. The full core source coverage cross-check remains open.
- Game Boy state mutations no longer commit and only afterward attempt to record an event. Empty-console power-on, intentional power-off (including the related virtual session event), cartridge move, insert, eject, and disappeared-emulator reconciliation group their state change and matching factual console events in one SQLite transaction; a rejected event rolls back the changes. The inventory synchronization no longer changes a cartridge's `updated_at` for every ordinary read if its title and location are unchanged. The real CLI lifecycle regression injects ignored event inserts and checks state rollback and unchanged timestamps.
- Audited the two attached Game Boy SQLite databases for power-loss semantics. An ordinary SQLite WAL transaction can roll back both attached files after a SQL error but **cannot guarantee an all-or-nothing crash commit across the files**. The console now switches both to verified `DELETE` rollback-journal mode with `synchronous=FULL` before any state mutation. SQLite's super-journal then provides the cross-file power-loss atomicity absent from WAL. Failure to switch the journal mode aborts rather than continuing with false assurances. The fixture confirms both active modes and explicitly migrates pre-existing WAL databases without deleting the legacy power, inventory or event history. This trades some WAL read/write concurrency for stronger two-database crash consistency.
- The separate `r2_diary.c` filesystem tools were still validating a realpath and then reopening it through `fopen` or `opendir`, allowing a directory entry to become an outside-workspace symlink in between. All four operations—directory listing, complete read, chunk read and local search—now resolve aliases under the workspace, descend from a pinned root through `openat` with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and verify the final descriptor's type. The listing classifies children using `fstatat` on its pinned directory; stream, directory scan and chunk-overflow errors fail instead of silently returning apparent success. The native diary integration fixture swaps a validated leaf and parent and injects stream/scan failures, following the production code path. A CI run exposed that its initial `openat`-only test wrapper missed an alternative libc call symbol; the fixture now intercepts `openat`, `openat64` and fortified variants. The strengthened diary step passed in the later complete candidate run up to that step before subsequent development commits superseded it.
- These changes remain on the unmerged draft PR #185. Do not interpret a passing headless GitHub build as verification of the installed Qwen model, GTX 1050 Ti offload, real databases, audio/video, physical TV/Game Boy, or overnight service restart; those require the target host. The current combined head requires a fresh full CI success after the final code/test changes and exact SHA reconciliation before release consideration.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-14-161 — Refuse model-store overlap, isolate shared blob inodes, and protect snapshot destinations

- **Reviewed:** the complete current `tools/copy_ollama_model.py`, `tests/copy_ollama_model_integrity.py`, `tools/audit_r2_databases.py`, and `tests/test_database_preflight.py`; additionally inspected the full `Observer.py`, the service/model runner shell paths, and the rollout-readiness documentation. These are scoped current reads, not a claim of reconciled 100% whole-tree coverage.
- **Model-store overlap:** the copier previously accepted the same model store as source and destination, or a source nested in the destination or vice versa. A mistaken option could turn the private-store `chown`/`chmod` operations into mutations of the original Ollama store. The copier now resolves both roots and rejects equal/ancestor/descendant paths **before reading or changing destination files**. Regressions cover equality, nested destinations, ancestor destinations, and normalized parent components, and verify the source manifest is unchanged.
- **Shared destination inodes:** an already-valid destination blob could be a hard link to another file. Metadata updates on that path would also change the other link, even when SHA-256 was correct. Existing destination blobs with more than one hard link are now replaced through the existing independently verified temporary-file/atomic-rename path before metadata updates. The native test verifies a shared external file retains its inode contents and permissions, while the destination becomes independent.
- **Snapshot path safety:** the read-only database auditor previously prevented overwriting existing snapshot filenames, but could traverse preexisting destination-directory symlinks. It now refuses symlink components on the chosen snapshot directory and each required optional-device parent before copy. New regressions cover a symlinked snapshot root and a symlinked optional Game Boy subtree, verifying no redirected snapshot creation.
- **Verification:** the full GitHub Actions `R2 build` workflow [43643087363](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/43643087363) passed **123/123** steps at exact code/test commit `8ac7573bf8747f32b0cd08e66ba7a4b068bbecc6`, including the modified model-copy integrity and database-preflight test suites. The individual preceding commits were superseded by this exact combined run; do not interpret cancelled intermediate push runs as failed code tests.
- **Boundaries:** this is repository validation of deterministic integrity scenarios. It does not establish the physical R2 host's installed Qwen model, mixed CPU/GPU placement, actual database state, microphone/camera/speaker/CRT/Game Boy, trusted privileged bootstrap, or overnight reliability. Keep PR #185 draft and `main` unchanged until `docs/rollout-readiness.md` checks pass.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-15-192 — Consolidated source inspection and CRT persistence fault recovery

- **Exact repository inventory:** reviewed the current consolidated Git tree at `8b7a8b9622fc2611b20ad4fa82d69cccb1974ddd`: **190 tracked blob files**, including **121 `tests/` files**. Inspected startup/failure/shutdown and conversation persistence/association ranges in `r2.c`, the real init-unwind fixture, `Log.c` archived conversation and indexed search paths, `r2_diary.c` workspace path/copy code, `GameBoyAdvance.py` DB journal/launch/acknowledgement paths, `Observer.py` and `V-Webcam.py` reader lifecycles, `tools/copy_ollama_model.py` and `tools/audit_r2_databases.py`, source/client headers, `Makefile`, service units and CI. - **Defect fixed in actual CRT/VCR implementation:** malformed persisted `position_seconds` values could raise `ValueError` or `OverflowError` during refresh/seek, potentially terminating Tk event callbacks. `TV.py` now rejects nonnumeric and non-finite stored positions, and values above the existing **604800-second** Reality/VCR protocol maximum; preserves the prior negative-to-zero clamping. Refresh and delayed playback callbacks gracefully handle invalid states instead of sending invalid milliseconds into VLC.
- **Exact database paths:** raw `file:${DB_PATH}?mode=ro` concatenation broke valid SQLite paths containing URI query/fragment delimiters. Both TV readers now construct escaped read-only URIs through `DB_PATH.resolve().as_uri() + "?mode=ro"`. Regression verifies a real SQLite path containing `?` and `#` remains readable, exact and unchanged.
- **Executable regressions:** expanded `tests/tv_display_smoke.py` with malformed, non-finite and extreme saved positions; the seven-day valid boundary; a GUI fallback callback; and a special-character SQLite path. The first full run failed because the newly authored fixture read another SQLite connection before the test's write transaction committed; corrected the fixture's commit boundary instead of changing product semantics. A follow-up source trace identified a finite-but-huge millisecond overflow, addressed by enforcing Reality's existing position ceiling before seeking.
- **Exact verification:** seven focused code/test commits after `8b7a8b9622fc2611b20ad4fa82d69cccb1974ddd`, touching only `TV.py` and `tests/tv_display_smoke.py` (no deletes/renames). ### R2-AUD-2026-11-15-193 — Close read-only CRT database handles on every poll

- **Source inspection:** traced `TV.py`'s `CRTDisplay.refresh`, `read_state`, `read_vcr_state`, and their recurring SQLite read-only connections. Followed related `Observer.py` connection ownership, `GameBoyAdvance.py` CLI close path, and `tools/audit_r2_databases.py` closure as comparisons. Read `AudioCapture.c` and `AudioFile.c` through their capture startup, decoder child, cancellation, read, EOF, and cleanup paths; inspected privileged model/installation and service-launch configurations for additional ownership faults. No unverified source changes were made outside the identified TV connection lifetime defect.
- **Defect:** Python's `sqlite3.Connection` context manager commits/rolls back transactions but does **not** close the handle. Both TV readers used `with sqlite3.connect(...)`, each opening a new read-only SQLite connection for the one-second display refresh cycle and depending on eventual garbage collection for closure. This could accumulate handles and unnecessarily pin read transactions on implementations without immediate refcount cleanup.
- **Fix:** use `contextlib.closing(sqlite3.connect(...))` in both TV reader methods. Retain read-only `mode=ro`, exact URL-escaped paths, exception behavior, SQL queries, and presentation logic.
- **Runtime regression:** `tests/tv_display_smoke.py` now uses a tracked proxy around the real SQLite connection and checks that three independent calls to each reader yield exactly six matching close operations. This exercises actual query behavior rather than only asserting source text.
- **Exact verification:** committed `0c83fd6c` and `f134ba5b` (only `TV.py` and `tests/tv_display_smoke.py`). GitHub Actions [run 43755455855](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/43755455855) **passed 128/128 steps at code/test commit `f134ba5b72eaaa5099905c3efc9712d22ac9cf81`**, including TV regression, production strict compilation, offline typed conversation/history restart, address/undefined-behavior sanitizers and isolated SIGTERM service lifecycle.
- **Audit boundaries:** this completes a scoped GUI connection-lifecycle correction, not the unreconciled historical 190-file whole-source manual inspection. The user-established host/model/peripheral baseline remains unchanged. Draft PR #187 continues the current development branch; `main` is untouched.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-20-267 — Translate conversation and visual inference to independent local models

- **Scope inspected:** `r2.h`, `r2.c` request construction/visual request gate/first-answer evidence, `Visual.c` model selection and current-frame inference, `Visual.h`, interactive and overnight launch/preflight/model-copy paths, shell model status, and model-specific regression assertions. This is a scoped trace, **not** an audit of the whole repository.
- **Starting state:** New branch `integrate/r2-llama31-qwen3vl-completion-2026-11-20` was created from PR #188's exact head `603afd8b860fb6bb96794cdfba070c2bd12287c7`, which already contains the history of PRs #185–#187. Draft PR #189 targets PR #188's branch. No merge or write to `main`.
- **Root incompatibility:** The old compiled configuration bound both conversation and vision to `qwen3.5:9b`. Choosing text-only `llama3.1:8b` without changing direct camera/media image serialization would send image-bearing chat messages to a language-only model. `Visual.c` also rejected an independent vision model.
- **Changes:** Conversation uses `llama3.1:8b`, and `Visual.c` independently uses `qwen3-vl:4b-instruct-q4_K_M`. Real camera and chosen media frames are independently encoded, interpreted by Qwen3-VL and supplied to Llama as source-labeled observational evidence. Original user messages retain their exact text and role. Missing image interpretation is not fabricated as sight. Existing visual archive/explicit observation systems remain in place. Foreground-owned visual requests queue on the existing shared Ollama gate rather than silently losing captured evidence when busy; background visual inference remains opportunistic. Prompts now name the actual source-labeled observation format.
- **Deployment:** Interactive and overnight paths identify both models. The installer copies both already-installed model manifests/blobs to R2's private Ollama store; no download occurs in the project. Preflight separately requires exact tags, tests Llama text and Qwen3-VL image inference, and inspects each model's GPU placement at the appropriate time. The trusted installer bootstrap's documented immutable commit, blob pin, and wrapper pin were reconciled after source changes.
- **Tests:** The earlier branch produced repeated strict-CI failures from stale one-model fixture expectations. These were updated, not disabled. The pre-fix run `44859098785` passed 50 steps before `media_focus_bridge_smoke.py` asserted the obsolete direct dual-image request; its replacement explicitly checks the translated camera and media evidence. Full CI on the final head remains **pending/unverified** at the time of this entry. The local Llama and Qwen3-VL models, live cameras/TV, persistent databases and physical GPU were not touched.
- **Open acceptance:** Run strict CI on the exact final head; inspect any real compilation/runtime failures, and verify serialization, source changes during slow visual inference, prompt/context capacity for appended visual evidence, real Llama conversational/structured actions, real Qwen vision output, RAM/VRAM/offload switching and overnight stability. These are **not** certified by the static bridge or earlier passing suites. Preserve every established subsystem and do not restore symptom-masking reply filters.

- **Reviewed source:** Draft #189 at `2318420e3213c8e58144a31312f96e1d4990122d`. Enumerated 191 tracked files and retrieved each file against its blob ID. Checked build and test wiring across the inventory. Detailed manual inspection focused on the runtime paths and failure cases below. This entry does not certify a line-by-line review of every file.
- **Conversation and model paths:** Followed `r2_input.h` and `shell.c` into the serialized public turn in `r2.c`. Checked exact current-turn and role selection, context capacity, Qwen frame translation, complete Llama replies, tool routing and fallback, foreground priority, post-turn scheduling and shutdown. The single display inspection result can reach the follow-up without replaying the action.
- **Sensory and device paths:** Traced the Watch coordinator, camera and display frame ownership, source revision and media attention, digital audio capture and decoder cancellation, visual archive publication, preview and renderer handoffs, Observer reads, and Game Boy process identity and journal boundaries.
- **Persistent state paths:** Reviewed Life Log turn and evidence staging, learning acknowledgement, diary and journal correlation by ID, cross-store purchase recovery, Reward and Addiction replay, and the Imagination and Alternate-Self evidence boundary. Reviewed launcher and service configuration against exact local model tags. Both shipped systemd units match the installer's embedded definitions. The bootstrap blob ID agrees with the installer wrapper and both deployment guides.
- **Open source finding:** In `vision_watch_capture_worker` (`r2.c:11929`), failure to copy frame metadata or allocate the latest-frame buffer can stop continuous capture after a valid frame while Eyes stays open. In `shell_watch_worker` (`shell.c:1629–1648`), a still-open camera owned by Watch keeps its camera token, and the worker-attachment branch requires that token to be absent. Direct foreground capture can still work after the worker exits. Automatic recovery from this failure remains open. This is a source trace without hardware reproduction.
- **Verification:** [Run 45277500624](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45277500624) passed all 128 steps on the reviewed commit, including restart, sanitizers and isolated SIGTERM. The download is pinned to that commit. Live reply quality, device and GPU placement on the actual host, and sustained operation still require physical acceptance. This audit only updates the ledger.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-272 — Resume capture on an open Watch-owned camera

- Closed the camera recovery finding in entry 271. In `shell_watch_worker` (`shell.c:1646`), Watch can start a replacement capture worker when a permitted camera is still open and the previous worker has exited. Source ownership and the replacement worker token remain separate. The camera stays open and keeps its existing owner during recovery.
- The native coordinator regression in `tests/watch_renderer_switch_runtime.c` simulates worker exit at the device boundary after Watch opens the camera. It drives the real polling loop and verifies a single camera open, a single capture restart, no release during recovery, and exact ownership cleanup at Watch stop. The test uses simulated devices.
- [Run 45288112087](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45288112087) passed all 128 steps on `ddfc70a7cafe891d4546a25397d95865492982d7`, including camera ownership, decoder lifecycle, offline conversation and restart, sanitizers, and isolated shutdown. Live hardware remains outside this result.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-273 — Wait for the first display frame on deliberate inspection

- Traced `r2_vision_media_see` from a deliberate inspection through the media worker, frame lock and source revision. A newly opened display could return an unavailable result before its first frame was published.
- The direct inspection now waits up to five seconds for the first complete frame of its display generation. Publication, source end, voluntary Eyes closure and shutdown wake waiting inspections. Ordinary passive capture and attention keep their existing behavior.
- The native visual lifecycle fixture gates synthetic decoder output until the inspection waits, then checks the complete frame reaches analysis without establishing sustained attention. It also stops a decoder while the inspection waits for its first complete frame and checks prompt cancellation.
- [Run 45291906635](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45291906635) passed all 128 steps on `5e4dd6ca3cb0740bd24dca0c978ae5ba3301d2db`, including visual lifecycle, offline conversation and restart, sanitizers and isolated shutdown. Live hardware remains outside this result.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-274 — Preserve the original typed turn under context pressure

- Traced terminal framing and shell dispatch through the full user-role JSON string and length-delimited loopback request. Overlong input and embedded NUL are rejected by the reader. The observed greeting does not request a visual source or enter the speech path.
- In compact foreground chat, a large same-turn result or state view could send a short user turn for staged review. The final request could then use review notes as its user message and count those notes as the exact user text in the audit. The code now reserves that fallback for input too large to fit beside the compact policy itself. The audit keeps the original reference and marks reviewed input separately. The regression fills the remaining context with a synthetic handoff and verifies that no review request replaces the current typed message.
- [Run 45306112198](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45306112198) passed all 128 steps on `fece2f2d161e3658c7ac4ad70dde54a53c67f384`, including the focused transport and context tests and offline restart. This scoped trace establishes the code path and fix. It does not identify the cause of the specific live reply or certify model behavior.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-275 — Bind follow-up requests to the latest human turn

- Traced follow-up selection through `r2_foreground_chat`, `ollama_chat_with_limit`, and the prompt audit. The request builder formerly continued into historical user messages after the newest human message disagreed with the authoritative query. The audit also scanned every user message for an exact match. A historical match could therefore conceal the mismatch.
- Both selectors now stop at the newest human message and skip internal tool results. The serializer refuses a mismatched turn. The audit checks the actual serialized active user message. The native regression exercises a stale expected query with a matching historical message, then verifies the real follow-up and its recorded tool result still reach the model endpoint.
- [Run 45308159353](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45308159353) passed all 128 steps at `d9175ba9dce13fed340bd345a49d46d1186ed4dc`, including offline restart and sanitizers. The regression verifies request selection with a simulated endpoint. Live reply quality remains outside this check.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-276 — Preserve source selection through long-turn review

- Traced the original user text and the reviewed text through compact foreground chat, capability ordering, live-source routing and request diagnostics. The reviewed text must still be used to match the user-role wire message. The original text now supplies capability relevance and the explicit camera or display request. Isolated requests retain their own query.
- The native regression checks both directions: a review that introduces a screen request cannot add display routing, and a review that omits an explicit camera request cannot hide the current camera availability notice.
- [Run 45314048678](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45314048678) passed all 128 steps on `783f37985648396a99935347e5f50dcd382e6d37`, including the focused request test, offline conversation and restart, sanitizers and shutdown. Live model behavior remains outside this check.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-277 — Carry visible fallback replies into the next turn

- Traced `r2_talk_impl` from failed generation and recorded tool fallback through live history, Life Log staging, and post-turn learning. Replies synthesized by the runtime were shown and archived but omitted from live history, so the next prompt could omit the answer the user had just seen.
- Live history now retains the visible fallback when the turn ends normally. The existing generation flag still controls learning and self-reports. Regressions verify exact file-read fallback under history pressure and the outgoing follow-up request after a display inspection fallback.
- [Run 45322904913](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45322904913) passed all 128 steps at `b6dd8b125ecf5d37e8cbdade86f42dfe776e75ad`, including offline restart, sanitizers and shutdown. Follow-up behavior was checked with simulated Ollama.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-278 — Preserve smaller exchanges under history pressure

- Traced both selection passes in `ollama_chat_with_limit` and `r2_preserve_inference_units`. A large recent reply could consume the preliminary byte allowance and exclude shorter candidates. The whole-exchange pass would then reject that large exchange after losing the ability to consider the shorter exchanges.
- The recent-message pass now selects candidates by count and leaves their byte allocation to the whole-exchange pass. The new native regression checks that the outgoing 2,048-token request retains a smaller complete exchange and the current question after skipping an oversized exchange. The static check verifies the boundary between these passes.
- [Run 45325318291](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45325318291) passed all 128 steps at `5b29a9b9269b49bc33bf85bbda7df3db7a865831`, including the request regression, offline restart, sanitizers and shutdown. The regression verifies the request using a simulated endpoint.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-23-279 — Reserve recent dialogue before optional snapshots

- Traced final message allocation in `r2_preserve_inference_units`. Optional startup memory and persistent learning snapshots formerly consumed bytes before the recent-exchange pass. A follow-up could lose the immediately preceding exchange even when it fit with all required inputs.
- The allocator now reserves required inputs, considers complete recent exchanges, and fits optional snapshots in the remaining space. Startup and learning snapshots retain precedence over the optional original archive. The regression covers a budget that fits only the required sections and the recent exchange, then checks that both snapshots return when the larger budget permits them.
- [Run 45329394233](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45329394233) passed all 128 steps at `7ca4b2eaaa42ec2b3f66b6b9f220e4878a6b7551`, including offline conversation, restart, sanitizers and shutdown. This pass verifies allocation and regression behavior with a simulated endpoint.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-280 — Answer when optional history review cannot fit

- Traced `r2_foreground_chat` and `r2_answer_after_failed_history_review` from compact prompt allocation through the final local request. Required input could fit exactly while optional historical material forced a review, whose heading or fallback notice could make the code abandon the answer.
- When the core turn fits, the optional-history path now keeps the exact message and required instructions, only including the notice and complete historical blocks that fit. The native test fills the 2,048-token request allocation with required input and verifies a single complete answer request with no review heading.
- [Run 45332674351](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45332674351) passed all 128 steps at `5eb30235b3e7d79ad4324b9ae9d7cb08599c15b9`, including request capture, offline conversation and restart, sanitizers and shutdown. The model endpoint is simulated in the regression.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-281 — Recover from an incomplete foreground model response

- Traced `ollama_chat_with_limit` and `ollama_submit_chat` from response framing through action handling. The serialized-request replay previously applied only to isolated calls, so a broken foreground response could force an otherwise intact current turn straight to its fallback.
- Foreground answers now use the same single byte-for-byte replay on an empty or malformed response or an eligible interrupted receive. Response-length limits and HTTP errors retain their existing handling. The replay takes place before any returned action text can be interpreted.
- [Run 45336381272](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45336381272) passed all 128 steps at `72bb60f00f1bc9031a22e2fddae48fc634a2fd2a`. The native tests check malformed JSON, partial-file and receive errors, bounded failure, and no replay on a generation limit using simulated Ollama. Offline restart, sanitizers and shutdown also passed.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-282 — Reject model output with an explicit non-stop completion reason

- Traced text generation, visual analysis, and startup readiness from non-streaming response parsing through acceptance. With `done=true` and an assistant message, an explicit reason such as `cancelled` or `load` previously let plausible text through.
- An explicit reason must now be `stop`. A response without the field still works for compatibility, and `length` retains its existing diagnostic. The text regression verifies rejection without replay. The vision regression verifies that no observation, pending archive, or image is published for cancelled output. The readiness tests exercise both probes with valid answers carrying non-stop or malformed reasons.
- [Run 45342910823](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45342910823) passed all 128 steps at `e08c6ebca19681c8064d3585e09027ace160af89`, including offline conversation and restart, sanitizers, and SIGTERM shutdown. These tests used simulated Ollama responses.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-283 — Replay an otherwise valid response with no final text

- Traced `ollama_submit_chat` and `ollama_chat_with_limit` in `r2.c` through `r2_talk_impl` and its post-action follow-up. An otherwise valid completion containing only private reasoning or whitespace bypassed the retry used for malformed or empty response bodies and went straight to the conversation fallback.
- An accepted completion with no visible final text now uses the same single serialized-request replay, before any reply or action can be published. The existing gate still limits it to foreground answers and isolated requests. Explicit non-stop completion, allocation failure while copying valid final text, and shutdown retain their previous handling.
- The native fixture compares both payloads byte for byte, tests private-reasoning-only and whitespace responses, and checks that a second blank answer stops replay. The post-action case preserves the original question and exact recorded result with a stale Reality snapshot at both 2,048 and 8,192 tokens.
- [Run 45468609528](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45468609528) passed all 128 steps at `9cb7789ae10cd1126d6e624c814a1ef0a8a08bd8`, including offline conversation and restart, sanitizers, and SIGTERM shutdown using simulated Ollama. The offline fixture also verifies that the action executes once and its result survives an empty first follow-up. The initial run 45467284445 exposed an outdated one-call expectation for exhausted blank answers. The revised test verifies exactly one replay and continued learning from the original user report. Live model output quality remains unverified by this scoped trace.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-284 — Count only retained exchanges against the recent history limit

- Traced `ollama_chat_with_limit` and `r2_preserve_inference_units` from the active-turn reservation through chronological serialization. The provisional recent-message limit could be filled by exchanges later rejected for size, leaving an earlier fitting exchange out even when both byte and message capacity remained.
- The final allocator reserves the active turn and attached action evidence first, then spends the remaining slots only on whole historical exchanges that fit. The native fixture checks recovery after eight oversized exchanges at 2,048 tokens and confirms the message cap still preserves the newest whole exchanges when they all fit.
- The first run, [45472215369](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45472215369) at `f55a7e89a9fa1fd9709aa20c98f3fa8a1c5b2205`, exposed static assertions tied to the removed provisional loop. Updated the guard to check the final allocator. [Run 45480352858](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45480352858) passed 128 steps at `e2fc141f6c81707e30b68ea600a2b45366822780` using simulated Ollama.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-285 — Validate UTF-8 before admitting a conversation turn

- Traced typed shell input, the direct terminal, and the speech-event wrapper to `r2_talk`. The terminal reader rejects embedded NUL and oversized lines, but undecodable UTF-8 could still reach message history and the Ollama serializer.
- The public conversation entry now rejects malformed UTF-8 before inference and persistence and returns a visible explanation. The byte check accepts Unicode scalar values verbatim and rejects malformed continuation bytes, overlong encodings, surrogate values, and values above the Unicode range.
- A standalone check compared 1,212,063 samples against Python's strict UTF-8 decoder. The native fixture checks the visible error and unchanged history and request counts. The full CI run above includes offline conversation, restart, sanitizers, and SIGTERM shutdown. This scoped pass does not verify live model conversation quality.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-286 — Reserve required turn instructions in the compact context

- Traced `r2_chat_with_input_source`, `r2_compact_foreground_chat`, and the final allocation in `r2_foreground_chat` through the exact user message and action handoff. The compact prepass counted the user and tool text before considering history, but delayed budgeting required system details until it visited them. Historical material listed first could therefore trigger extra review even with enough room for the active turn and recent exchange.
- Required compact system details, including input provenance, action phase, and unavailable-state notices, are reserved before sizing reviews and selecting historical evidence. Explicit recall and the final allocator retain their existing roles.
- The 2,048-token native fixture puts oversized historical context first and verifies one request containing the exact current question, source label, stale-state instruction, and complete preceding exchange. [Run 45483127902](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45483127902) passed 128 steps at `4b7593120832e8cb58653c47cde81025100b61e3` with simulated Ollama, including restart, sanitizers, and SIGTERM shutdown. This scoped pass leaves live model reply quality unverified.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-287 — Verify desktop startup before applying runtime network isolation

- Traced the interactive launcher from existing-process checks through the UID-scoped network policy, hardware preflight, five-store audit, local model probes, strict compile, and core handoff. The hardware helper checked the private Ollama endpoint as `r2` after that user was restricted to port 11434. A separately running endpoint on port 11435 could therefore escape the check and compete for the same GPU.
- The desktop launcher now checks service state and the second loopback endpoint before applying the runtime network policy. It exits with a visible explanation on conflict. Standalone hardware checks, SQLite checks, and generation probes retain their existing scope.
- Bash syntax and three isolated shell cases passed. An active systemd service and a responding standalone endpoint each stop startup, while an unavailable endpoint permits startup to continue. [Run 45486267065](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45486267065) passed 128 steps at `8aa32a6478072fd18d6f5f60674f52947677423d`, including the strict build, peripheral-free offline conversation and restart, database checks, sanitizers, and SIGTERM shutdown. No target host or real Ollama installation was available in this workspace. First production launch remains subject to the on-host probes already built into the launcher.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-288 — Separate the desktop account home from the R2 project directory

- Traced the interactive launcher and standalone hardware preflight through the privilege drop, working directory, data paths, and audio discovery. PulseAudio emitted a home-ownership error because the desktop-launch handoff set the application's Linux home to the project directory owned by `x`.
- The desktop launcher and preflight use `/home/r2` as the Linux home and retain the established project path `/home/x/R2_Home`. The report warns when the runtime account does not own its Linux home or cannot traverse or write it. No permissions or account records were changed on the target host.
- Bash syntax and the isolated hardware-preflight and launcher-order fixture passed. Run 45498081844 exposed a desktop HOME assertion in the overnight harness. Updated the desktop assertion and retained the existing service HOME expectation. [Run 45499130776](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45499130776) passed 128 steps at `747e5d061ff7782c88682d72f7fa5134a6f4373f`. The observed filesystem exposed the Linux home's owner as UID 65534 and the runtime user's UID as 1001. Host ownership and live audio therefore remain to be verified.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-289 — Preserve the immediately preceding live exchange at the actual inference allocation

- Traced typed input through `shell_dispatch`, `shell_talk_inner`, `r2_talk`, `r2_talk_impl`, live history append, Reality replacement, associative planning, retrieval snapshot, `r2_chat_with_input_source`, full and compact foreground policies, whole-unit preservation, ordering and JSON serialization. The first outward answer is appended before the shell returns; its durable learning does not have to complete before a second turn. Actual on-host prompt capture was not available in this session.
- The full-policy selector previously checked the current required input without the preceding exchange; the compact path used the originally requested output quota instead of the serializer's clamped quota. A selected identity or capability block could then consume the remaining bytes reserved for dialogue. Explicit recall classification could also prioritize historical retrieval over the immediately preceding exchange (including ordinary wording such as "you said you"). The staged-review path had the same capacity conflict.
- Commits `d72c5f99da19ba645d88569039e0fa63a70750e9`, `1df586f359552dec5e3ec361c46fb5a72e90228a`, and `f695ea11339aa9cf4a556416ec30ed0b001ad0ae` reconcile policy selection, generation allocation, complete exchange slot accounting and staged-review capacity. The serializer refits generation allocation for the preceding complete exchange when the required input and exchange fit the actual configured context with the existing generation floor. Original historical records, actual user bytes and action handoffs are retained in their stores; explicit recall can still select complete records for the existing staged review. The final focused retrieval notice is measured against the remaining allocation in `18e8c910e7591af504745c57fd9553068ba8fa6a`.
- Native runtime regression additions in `f3ea692b84c4d28bc21cef8a3827b2613f5ed2cc` and `61b195b6491589dae20f74309cdb3c047797522d` inspect exact message contents/roles and final output reservation in simulated Ollama requests at 2048 and 4096 context tokens, including explicit recall. Earlier intermediate workflow runs 45522327111, 45522591679 and 45522639828 were cancelled as the branch advanced; [run 45522925122](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45522925122) at the last code/test commit was in progress when checked. No successful compilation or test result is claimed for the new work yet. Physical model output, exact tokenizer capacity and actual prompt latency remain unverified.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-290 — Preserve a scheduled reflection when the inference slot is temporarily occupied

- Traced `autonomous_thinking`, its idle/reflection timer, `generate_with_private_evidence`, shared Ollama request mutex, foreground cancellation, diary write and post-turn worker queue. A scheduled reflection previously used the same nonblocking request acquisition as opportunistic bookkeeping. A busy mutex could return no inference result and consume the entire 900-second reflection interval despite no diary inference being submitted.
- Commit `18e8c910e7591af504745c57fd9553068ba8fa6a` makes the scheduled reflection worker wait for the existing inference owner during idle time and observe the existing foreground/shutdown cancellation path while waiting. Ordinary post-turn workers continue to defer and use their durable queue; no additional model request or diary-generation retry is introduced. Existing model, memory stores, action validation and reflection cadence are unchanged.
- Commit `61b195b6491589dae20f74309cdb3c047797522d` adds native contention and foreground-interruption fixtures. Their CI outcome is pending at the same checkpoint above. This is a scoped source inspection and correction, not proof of an end-to-end overnight run. The memory evaluation path distinguishes a valid NONE from failed generation and queues durable pending work; no actual memory-decision output or target database was inspected, so the reported lack of new memories is not yet attributed to a single verified cause. Future inspection should trace context-review progress and model output for that stage, verify actual second-turn payload and diary completion on the installed model, and resolve any CI failures before deployment.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-291 — Correct conversation Life Log allocation before post-turn learning

- Manually traced `r2_talk_impl` finalization through `r2_log_conversation_turn_for_learning`, `log_conversation_turn_with_evidence`, pending conversation/archive publication, `log_stage_learning_locked`, `log_stage_self_report_locked`, the model memory-decision parser, `run_post_turn_job`, `replay_pending_learning` and `r2_log_save_learning`/memory write transaction.
- Confirmed an off-by-one heap write: `details_size` excludes the terminating NUL but the final copy includes it, both when no tool evidence is present and when the tool-result suffix is present. Commit `6a2c1c11e9583de04f024eb352164e7a6017c521` allocates the full string including its terminator; all original text, transaction ordering and persistent schema are preserved. No retries or fallback behaviors are introduced.
- Commit `398a6ddb2a0615353476555128cf130209b16658` extends the native request fixture with exact Life Log and pending-learning/self-report comparisons for long exchanges both with and without tool evidence. Commit `21be09d137b569e63cacb77bb8b574e4d3f14c09` adds a deterministic allocation-size regression using the existing malloc wrapper. It refuses the former undersized allocation and requires the corrected path to stage the event successfully.
- Prior run `45523026243` completed the native real-request allocation/transport/incomplete-output step successfully, including audit 289–290 fixtures, plus 42 successful steps in total, before cancellation as the branch advanced. Full CI for this allocation repair has not completed. The physical R2 process was not accessed; this proven C defect is a plausible contributor to the reported second-turn or persistence problem, not a verified explanation of the specific target-host incident.
- Additional findings requiring separate scoped investigation: an invalid memory-decision response remains pending, and the durable replay scheduler is currently driven by the autonomous reflection worker; stopping reflection can also delay queued learning. The Life Log's "Memory indexed" field refers to its separate event-pointer indexing operation, not necessarily the presence of an evaluated memory linked to a conversation. No memory-selection policy or indexing semantics were changed.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-292 — Correct prior allocation attribution, separate diagnostics regression, and independent learning dispatch

- Rechecked the arithmetic in audit 291 against the pre-change `Log.c` source. **Correction:** the original `sizeof(reply_prefix)` already included the terminating NUL; it was not a heap-buffer overflow. The extra allocated byte introduced by 6a2c1c1 was redundant. Commit `e3b877e30be95709a6c3b0332544541f669e5018` explicitly subtracts the terminator from both prefix lengths and allocates text length plus one. The effective allocation matches the pre-audit code. The boundary fixtures remain valuable byte-integrity checks but are not evidence of a historical overflow. The actual cause of the target-host second-turn/persistence symptoms cannot be inferred from that allocation.
- Run [45523662892](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45523662892) at da64d03 passed the native real-request allocation fixture, context-record read/size fixture and complete recall/reflection fixture. It failed at step 61, `tests/console_diagnostics_isolation_smoke.py`: the assertion still expected the old argument `R2_LIVE_DIAGNOSTICS` to be passed directly rather than the separate-window launcher's derived `R2_MIRROR_DIAGNOSTICS`. Source inspection confirmed the default stderr append and opt-in private mirror routes. Commit `9ce33b91ea88375acb97dac4ce92243757a03b84` updates the test to cover actual launcher wiring without changing the runtime launcher or user-facing stdout.
- Traced `schedule_post_turn_processing`, `finish_post_turn_worker`, `resume_post_turn_learning`, `replay_pending_learning`, `autonomous_thinking`, `r2_start_thinking`, `r2_stop_thinking`, `r2_init`, shutdown and startup-failure handling. Existing queue dispatch was polled exclusively by the autonomous diary thread after the initial main-level sweep. A stopped diary worker consequently prevented later accepted queued conversations from being dispatched. Commit `39fffc2cf9adf5f935e8d7fedfe88aad6c0faac1` moves the existing one-second flag poll and periodic durable sweep into a core-owned joinable learning scheduler. It retains the same queue, active-job reservation, foreground precedence, existing periodic interval and inference/decision policy; archive and diary-mirror replay remain with their existing subsystem. The scheduler is joined before the post-turn workers are drained and stores close. Worker count includes the new independent scheduler.
- Commit `137a510b123919328a5c94c79742e54b64a207d6` adds a native fixture using the actual scheduler, SQLite learning outbox and mock model bridge with reflection disabled; it stages a learning event during an owned foreground boundary and requires the accepted event to be learned after release. Commit `3c22bd851d4f5392ff34439fd261bcc7c2ca4861` acknowledges unrelated learning staged by the earlier structured-report-only fixture to isolate queue accounting. Commits `0b39f6a94fcbb0693e8ac66a88437258396829f8` and `6b26f56c88e7e5ac8ee264fb9facd8444002b4a4` exercise scheduler creation failure through the same centralized startup unwind and wire it into CI.
- The new scheduler, diagnostic assertion and corrected allocation representation are committed but full strict CI at the new head is not yet verified. The independent scheduler was selected to correct ownership/lifecycle rather than adding more inference retries. No changes to original memory data, model weights, context policy, private evidence rules or diary cadence. Physical prompt and memory-decision output, real database state and sustained target-host operation remain unavailable. Further work must inspect new CI output, fix any genuine failures and verify actual second-turn/learning/diary operation before declaring the issue resolved.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-293 — Use one saved-evidence scope for direct and staged memory evaluation

- Rechecked `memory_decision`, `generate_with_private_evidence`, `r2_review_context` (Imagination.c), `r2_model_generate`, `ollama_chat_with_limit`, response parsing, foreground cancellation, `preserve_evidence_memory` and the Life Log transaction in `r2_log_save_learning`. The observed log wording "no new persistent memory was saved" in the current source belongs to the null/invalid decision path, not the normal NONE acknowledgment. An actual host log matching this wording and the raw decision output were not available.
- The short memory-decision branch set its isolation/stage only for the direct model call; the oversized branch performed its final evaluation without that same scope. In addition, the direct branch did not clear an inherited authoritative user query. The shared serializer can reject an unrelated authoritative query as absent, or attach current sensory context when not isolated. Commit `887783521d4311ca42fedc7114bd31344fa8ae38` binds both memory paths to the same private saved-evidence scope, clears unrelated live query/sensory state during that evaluation, and restores all four thread-local values when the call finishes. It leaves the existing memory decision grammar, identity/evidence rules, durable writes, model and context limit unchanged. The reviewed final request now has the same existing isolated-request transport behavior as the direct one; no new retry algorithm is introduced. Ordinary post-turn workers normally begin with empty live-query TLS, so this scope mismatch is a source-confirmed inconsistency, not a proven cause of the reported production failures.
- Commit `7c8c5455a2490af56b14f39b615a00c53aeb654e` adds native coverage with a deliberately unrelated authoritative live query and sensory state, a direct decision, and a staged long saved reply at 4096 context tokens. It checks full original evidence across the review fragments, the archive reference, final decision, and restoration of the caller scope.
- Run [45524758256](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45524758256) at audit 292 head passed 51 steps, including native request allocation, independent learning dispatch with diary stopped and the context-record regression, before cancellation as the branch advanced. Run [45525277171](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45525277171) at the new test commit was beginning when checked. Full CI, actual installed-model output and target database acceptance remain outstanding.
- The currently inspected transaction commits a valid memory, its provenance link and pending-row acknowledgment together; valid NONE acknowledges the pending decision without creating a memory. An invalid response leaves the original evidence pending. No evidence supports forcing a memory record for every exchange or changing the model's decision criteria. Next work: verify the new fixture in CI and distinguish actual runtime evaluation failure from a valid NONE and SQLite write failure using the existing logs and durable records.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-295 — Inspect latest integration head and unblock strict compilation

- Inspected the exact latest integration head `57b9de1c8b4ab78910e6ddc6697912e0c01b51b6` and its 28 commits after the prior audit baseline. Reviewed the eight changed files and traced the affected source paths: `r2.c` complete-message allocator, output reservation and preceding exchange, compact/full foreground selection, associative planner/history ownership, diary request contention, learning scheduler and startup/shutdown, memory decision and saved-evidence scope; `Log.c` conversation byte accounting, pending rows and learning transaction; the related native and static fixtures and workflow wiring. GCC with `-Werror=stringop-overread` rejects `memchr(text, '|', (size_t)(end - text))` in `memory_decision`; the workflow step name about Whisper WAV conversion does not identify the source of the failure. Here `text` points into a NUL-terminated model response and `end` backs over trailing whitespace, so searching to the original terminator for a pipe has the same classification result. Replaced the computed-span call with `strchr(text, '|')`, preserving exact rejected output and its provenance event. No memory-policy or transport changes were made.

This workspace lacks SQLite/json-c/Pulse/cURL development headers, so the native compile and runtime fixtures require the strict GitHub CI runner. A passing full workflow, installed-model behavior and physical-host observation are not yet claimed.

The test searched for the action's text anywhere among user messages; the newly retained preceding exchange also appears in a later action request, so this counted another turn. The sanitizer and overnight SIGTERM jobs were skipped after the failing step. Full CI for this test correction is pending; the physical host is outside this inspection's verification scope.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-296 — Retain follow-up antecedents when associative planning fails

- Traced a normal shell message through `shell_dispatch`/`shell_talk_inner`, `r2_talk`, `r2_talk_impl`, live message append, `chat_with_relevant_memories`, `r2_build_associative_search_query`, Ollama response parsing, compact/full foreground allocation, visible reply append, Life Log staging and the independent post-turn worker. The first reply is appended before the next turn; the later worker does not own or rewrite the current conversation. Examined the creator's saved diagnostic trace separately from the source: one later planner request received malformed UTF-8 JSON twice, used the literal-query fallback, then submitted a foreground answer request. The trace also shows a 1024-to-192-token answer reservation on that older build; it does not by itself prove why a particular visible reply was incoherent or that the currently edited source was installed there.
- Source defect: `r2_associative_previous_exchange` copied the preceding human-facing user/reply pair for the planner, but `r2_build_associative_search_query` freed both copies before handling a failed/empty planner result. The fallback then searched only the newest user text. For a follow-up such as "Could you elaborate on that?", the retrieved memory/diary/Life Log searches lacked the antecedent even though it was already available in RAM. The new fallback places the exact newest query first and appends both exact preceding visible messages as read-only retrieval terms; it caches that search view for the rest of the turn. It never changes the authoritative user message, persists no inferred fact, executes no action and adds no model call. Allocation failure still uses the original query alone.
- Extended the native planner fixture to return malformed JSON on both allowed attempts. It asserts the exact current query and preceding visible pair are present in fallback search text, internal tool syntax is absent, and cached reuse performs no additional inference. Native compilation and runtime execution of this addition are pending; the installed model and physical host are outside this source trace.
### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-297 — Keep the antecedent searchable after a valid planner reply

- Followed typed input through `shell_talk_inner`, `r2_talk_impl`, the preceding visible pair, associative planning, keyword ranking, Life Log/diary retrieval and final conversation allocation. The valid planner path copied the preceding exchange into its model request but built the search text from only the current user message and generated concepts. A syntactically valid yet generic planner reply therefore made a direct follow-up lose the earlier subject during database lookup. The separate failed-planner path had already been corrected in audit 296.
- The successful path now puts the exact current user text first, the preceding visible user/reply pair second, and generated concepts last. This affects read-only search cues; it does not rewrite the live user turn or treat the prior assistant answer as verified external evidence. The allocation failure path also retains the previous pair. The native request fixture deliberately returns valid but generic cues and checks that the earlier subject remains in the bounded search terms, with the internal tool request excluded.
- Local `current_turn_context_smoke.py`, `context_integration_smoke.py`, `final_context_budget_smoke.py` and `git diff --check` passed. Native fixture compilation and the strict workflow remain pending for this edit; the observed installed-model reply is not attributed to this source defect without a matching host trace.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-298 — Report relevant memories omitted by the focused byte budget

- Traced the oversized retrieved-context branch in `r2_compact_foreground_chat` to its second `get_relevant_memories` call and the `partial` flag used to tell the answer model that search evidence is incomplete. Matching memory records over the focused byte budget were skipped before scoring; selected matching records that exhausted the remaining byte budget were also skipped silently. The compact answer could therefore receive no matching record and no incomplete-search notice, although the full record remained in SQLite.
- Moved the single-record budget check after keyword scoring so unrelated large memories do not imply a gap; a matching omitted record now sets `partial`. A ranked matching record that cannot fit alongside earlier selected records also sets `partial`. The unbounded normal retrieval retains its original selection. The native ranking fixture exercises a matching large record, multiple matching records competing for a small byte budget, and a genuinely absent match.
- This fixes the completeness signal only. It does not split, summarize, or erase the omitted memory; a compact turn still answers from the evidence that fits and explicitly acknowledges incomplete retrieval. Native build/runtime and full strict CI for this change are pending.
- [Strict run 45535445759](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45535445759) compiled and passed the native request fixture and 84 preceding checks, then failed the new ranking assertion for the supposedly absent query `no matching memory exists`. The existing oversized fixture memory literally contains `memory`, so the new completeness flag correctly reported that matching row as omitted. Changed that control query to a unique absent token; this is a test expectation correction. The ranking fixture and later jobs require a new completed run.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-300 — Optional snapshot eligibility lost before final allocation

- Read the source at baseline `63104b869dffb099b4ddd655e2db66220f23ea94` (tree `6fe6a4e...`) before creating this branch. Traced `chat_with_relevant_memories`, `r2_foreground_chat`, compact selection, `ollama_chat_with_limit`, required-input fitting, `r2_preserve_inference_units`, message ordering and serialization. This is a scoped manual source audit, not a whole-repository completion or installed-model claim.
- Confirmed defect in baseline `r2.c:4524–4584, 4101–4113`: the preliminary category pass stops when its provisional system budget is full; the final whole-message allocator then refuses to consider optional system snapshots unless that preliminary pass had set their include bits. Its actual input capacity can exceed the preliminary system budget, including after output reservation is reallocated. An intact startup, learning or archive snapshot can therefore fit after the complete live dialogue yet be silently omitted. Optional snapshots must be selected by the final byte budget, independently of provisional category eligibility; do not displace required instructions or live dialogue.
- Status: confirmed, repair and regression pending at the time of logging.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-301 — Focused retrieval misreports completeness across source types

- Confirmed defect in baseline `r2.c:6893–7027, 5587–5655`: normal retrieved evidence includes persistent memory, diary, Life Log and other linked sources. If that combined block is too large, compact selection reconstructs only ranked persistent-memory records. It sets the incomplete notice only when that narrower database query reports partial/failure, even though the combined block was already omitted. Additionally the row budget can consume the notice's space when insufficient space remains to reserve it. A follow-up can then treat omitted historical evidence as absent.
- Repair scope: retain complete ranked records that fit alongside an explicit incomplete-source notice, reserving the notice first; if there is space only for a notice, retain it without performing a pointless narrow query. Do not fabricate, shorten or delete original evidence. If the exact live input leaves no room even for the notice, record that limit in diagnostics rather than overriding the live turn. This does not claim that a compact window can carry all combined source records.
- The shared four-way integer division at baseline `r2.c:3862–3884` distributes every remainder byte. The native boundary fixture at `tests/inference_request_runtime.c:357+` explicitly covers current allocation plus one byte and the maximum generation-floor boundary. Those arithmetic checks are distinct from the two eligibility/completeness defects above. Native execution has not been performed in this session.
- Status: confirmed, repair and regression pending at the time of logging.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-303 — Account for the actual bytes joining system sections

- Source inspection at code blob `abb2d1344f0812de69b7d4e45d980d66f1028824`: `ollama_json_chat_messages` joins system sections with exactly two newline bytes for every section after the first. Required-input measurement, final optional selection, full/compact preselection, focused records/notices and historical-review fallback currently omit these bytes. The nonvisual wire path also never measures the final joined content. For a 2048-token context and 64-token generation the estimated input allowance is 4710 bytes; two system sections and user text summing to 4710 produce 4712 actual content bytes. This is an arithmetic inconsistency independent of the tokenizer approximation or heap terminators.
- Repair the selection and measurement contract consistently, charge the actual delimiters, and test exact and one-byte-short boundaries. Do not change model safety settings, storage limits or add model requests merely to solve the arithmetic discrepancy.
- The startup memory row format has seven literal bytes; ordinary retrieved rows have four; their existing budget checks match those formats. The CURL buffer includes the terminating NUL, and live message storage includes both role and content terminators. These are distinct from inference content bytes. A fresh local Git checkout attempt failed with DNS resolution for github.com; source inspection is via the GitHub connection. Native runtime validation remains pending.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-304 — Text bridge mismeasures workspace reads with embedded NUL bytes

- Confirmed in `r2_diary.c` at blob `3631e5d744e08decabcba04e82cbca18c4400765`: the low-level chunk reader reports the actual length and next offset, but `r2_workspace_read_chunk_tool` uses strlen(result) to allocate and place metadata. Embedded NUL bytes therefore cause the returned text to omit subsequent bytes while the metadata advances across them. FILE_READ likewise passes an embedded-NUL buffer to a text-only interface.
- Correct the text bridge using the returned byte count and exactly measured metadata length. Keep the binary-capable low-level APIs intact; explicitly report when bytes cannot be represented in this C-string interface rather than returning misleading content or offsets. Test raw binary reads, ordinary text, and EOF.
- Status: confirmed before repair.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-305 — Workspace listing/search output cap uses estimated rather than exact row sizes

- The bounded listing and search builders use conservative fixed additions to estimate row sizes before formatting, rather than measuring the actual format. A final row that would fit can be omitted; near the output cap the omission notice may itself not fit. This is a completeness/accounting issue, not an established heap overflow.
- Status: open. Measure actual formatted sizes and reserve/report the omission notice without changing the established output cap. Do not confuse the intentional cap with the arithmetic defect.

After correcting the harness's own loop boundary, 3,157,162 checks passed. This is not the full R2 build, actual model tokenizer verification, or hardware testing.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-307 — Workspace search treats buffer fragments as complete lines

- Traced `r2_workspace_search` at source blob `5b4ee1c0e9d6ba5a94799ff606b278fc7d73a77e`: fgets reads at most 8191 bytes per call, yet the search increments its physical line number for each call and searches each fragment independently. A term crossing a fragment boundary is missed, subsequent line numbers are wrong, and a match returns only the fragment. Embedded NUL also makes the string search and formatter lose bytes. The read API already distinguishes binary and text; the search should likewise not misrepresent binary content.
- Repair together with audit 305: use complete dynamically allocated text lines and exact formatted record sizes. Preserve the existing 1 MiB record-content allocation, with the omission marker separately allocated only if a further record cannot fit; do not reserve it at the expense of an otherwise fitting record. Preserve workspace access checks. If a directory entry cannot be classified, do not claim a complete listing.
- Status: confirmed before edits.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-308 — Complete workspace allocation repairs without reducing capacity

- Audits 305 and 307 implemented in `dbe455a8606eef321e7581eb53b4dcea84ab0218` and `752938c4c4b61feb1c26c771623f4d70e2e8fc97`. Replaced duplicate approximate row sizing with one exact formatted append helper. It measures the record, reserves the terminating byte, grows allocation as needed and commits only a whole record. The established 1 MiB output allocation (1 MiB minus one content byte for NUL) is not reduced. An actual omission allocates its marker separately, without removing an already fitting record. Existing workspace authorization and descriptor-based path handling are unchanged.
- Search now uses complete physical lines, detects embedded NUL rather than silently treating a prefix as the entire line, and checks stream errors even when a partial read returned bytes. Directory classification errors no longer silently omit an entry. This is an interface accounting repair; source files are never edited by these operations.
- Native diary fixture `44c0b002037aaa8810d1ee4193aff2037b78ce2f` covers an exact 1 MiB-minus-NUL search result, an additional result requiring the omission marker, a match across the previous 8191-byte fragment boundary, subsequent physical line numbering and binary input. Updated the existing stream-error injection to wrap getdelim in `1d5ddbe86c56aab910d659f627887d5f224acba2`.
- Locally compiled an isolated harness using the three updated production functions, a fixture-only file opener, strict warnings, AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer. Passed 10,001 tested helper limits (0 through 10,000), exact and over-limit search outputs, cross-fragment matching, physical line numbering, embedded NUL rejection, empty and nonempty listing, and exact/over-limit directory listing with approximately 4,000 fixture files. This did not test the full application or its production authorization implementation; those remain covered by existing native fixtures.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-309 — Startup snapshot reserves unused text capacity and uses unrelated wrapper padding

- Source blob `e5965508fb6151d152250ecc40a46d8cc55deb82`: `get_startup_memories` at 1738–1855 permanently subtracts 256 bytes from the startup record allocation even when no status note is needed. A complete record that fits the configured startup budget can be skipped. The caller at 16444–16503 then independently allocates the snapshot plus 2048 spare bytes for a fixed wrapper, without checking formatted length. These are two distinct layers, not model context tokens or a reason to reduce the configured model window.
- Repair: allow complete rows to use the existing startup allocation including its actual NUL byte. Format any actual status notice separately using exact sizes; account for its additional bytes in the returned snapshot and use an exactly formatted wrapper. The existing final whole-message allocator measures the complete wrapped snapshot including its delimiter, and remains the authority on whether it fits the model request. Keep the 100-record selection limit, all database text, current-turn retrieval, configured model context and output reservation unchanged. No silent truncation.
- Add regressions for a record that fills the startup byte allocation exactly, a one-byte-too-large record, simultaneous omitted/unreadable records, and the wrapper's exact preservation.
- Status: confirmed before repair.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-310 — Private evidence review triggered by unused fixed framing reservation

- Source at `generate_with_private_evidence` in r2.c: the direct private-evidence payload is one complete system policy and one user message containing either context alone or context + two newline bytes + current input. It nevertheless subtracts a fixed 256 bytes from the available capacity before deciding whether to review. This can cause extra model calls and loss of direct verbatim evidence even when the entire original payload fits with the existing generation allocation. Review framing is only needed when a review is actually required.
- Use direct exact byte accounting first. For the review path alone, measure the actual archive-reference format using the longest valid signed 64-bit positive ID; charge current input and two newline bytes when applicable. Preserve the existing context and generation policies, review provenance, archived originals and safety constraints. Test direct exact fit, one-byte excess requiring review, and separate current-input framing.
- Status: confirmed before repair.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-24-311 — Startup and private-evidence allocation repair checkpoint

- Audit 309 repaired in `e766cc3af51276a30d798e80969c5a1745dd8909` and `bb951e4c6d643bdf7496fa51a5651d1c207ca025`. Complete startup rows can use the existing record allowance up to the terminating NUL. Actual status notices grow that buffer only when necessary; the exact measured notice lengths and terminator are checked at the write. The startup wrapper is dynamically formatted without unrelated fixed padding. The final request continues to measure the complete wrapped snapshot and system separator, with required and live input taking precedence. Its finite context may still exclude an intact optional snapshot; no stored records are removed.
- Audit 310 repaired in `43c5020b29ac3436131b4da37fdfce24e6f3795c`. Direct private evidence is measured against the existing output reservation using exact policy, context, current text and separator bytes. ### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-312 — Imagination input budget diverges from actual text inference

- At 2048 context tokens the kernel caps requested 700-token imagination output at 512; Imagination calculates its input from 700 anyway. Its estimate is 2675 bytes versus the actual 3276 bytes, losing 601 bytes before the serializer sees them. The evidence-review producer also permanently subtracts 512 bytes of formatting headroom despite the actual framing being much shorter. These cause avoidable review passes or refusal, not a heap-overrun. Trace: imagination request review -> evidence collection -> staged review -> r2_model_generate -> r2_ollama_predict_budget -> final JSON serializer.
- Use one shared pure text-inference budget calculation, keep the existing model window, output ceilings, 512-token template margin, and byte/token estimate. Fit the complete formatted chunk by measuring its actual reference fields, keeping UTF-8 boundaries and full review trace; update build/preflight dependencies and both mock and native regressions.
- Prior full run [45549950515](https://github.com/Xylin48/R2-3PO/actions/runs/45549950515) succeeded with all 128 steps, including the new startup/private-evidence fixtures and sanitizer integration.
- Status: confirmed before repair.


### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-313 — Reality TV status cannot represent some preserved legacy device identities

- Traced `r2_reality_tv_status`, its callers in r2.c and shell.c, and legacy migration. Live device connection limits new names to 120 bytes; migration and the database schema intentionally preserve existing identities without that limit. A valid legacy connected device with a name longer than 8192 bytes makes the fixed 8192-byte status buffer fail, even though the database preserved the record. The shell and model tool-result append paths accept dynamically allocated strings; the raw socket status route sends the whole returned string. TV.py does not request status and its separate bounded command-response protocol is unchanged.
- Repair the formatter to measure exact record lengths and expand its allocation. Check text fields for embedded NUL to avoid reporting a prefix as a complete identity. Preserve normal connection rules, media paths and device state. Also replace arbitrary spare bytes in fridge, room and combined Reality context formatting with exact dynamic formatting, keeping every existing heading and record.
- Add a native legacy-sized device fixture exceeding 8192 bytes and confirm its full name and following tape state are returned; check malformed embedded NUL text is not silently accepted.
- Status: confirmed before repair.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-316 — Generic follow-up terms consume historical search slots

- Continued the November 24 second-turn path at the latest draft #192 head `e1fbb79b`: `r2_talk_impl` → `chat_with_relevant_memories` → the associative planner and its exact previous-exchange fallback → `extract_memory_keywords` → six-search diary/visual collectors. A follow-up such as “Could you please explain and elaborate in more detail on that?” puts five generic request terms before the previous answer. ### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-317 — Ranked memory selection hides matching omissions

- Traced `get_relevant_memories` through its caller `chat_with_relevant_memories` and the compact focused-retrieval fallback. Ranking scans every saved memory and keeps a bounded highest-scoring selection. A relevant row below that window, or one displaced by a stronger later row, was silently discarded without setting the existing `partial` result flag. The caller could cache that result as complete and omit the retrieval-status notice; a later answer could infer absence from a deliberately bounded search. No stored memory was deleted.
- Mark both ranking omissions as partial while preserving the existing score, timestamp and ID ordering and every selected record's bytes. The existing current-turn status and focused incomplete-evidence notice can now express that more relevant memories were found. Extended the native ranking fixture to require partial at a three-of-four selection and complete at a four-of-four selection. The configured selection limits remain unchanged.

Full native build and integration remain pending in CI; the local environment lacks SQLite, JSON-C and PulseAudio development headers. This is a scoped source trace, not a claim that all historical stores are exhaustively searched.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-322 — Bounded Life Log matches appear complete in a follow-up

Each query returns at most three matching base events, but the batch previously had no completeness signal. If a fourth matching event existed, R2 received the first three with no notice that the search was bounded. This could turn omitted prior conversation evidence into an apparent absence.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-323 — Linked Life Log expansion silently omits related records

- Continued tracing `query_events` after the base-event cap. Link expansion scans at most four edges per base, selects at most two distinct neighbors per base and eight overall. Skipped distinct neighbors were not marked incomplete; once eight were selected, later base events were not inspected for more neighbors. A follow-up could interpret selected history as all related history.
- Structured retrieval now probes a fifth edge, marks skipped unseen neighbors incomplete at the per-event and global caps, and checks later base events after the global cap for unseen neighbors. The unstructured search preserves its selected output. The linked-retrieval fixture covers two-of-five linked neighbors, a one-link exact fit and eight-of-ten neighbors across five base events. The five-edge scan remains bounded and can conservatively report incomplete if its fifth edge duplicates already selected evidence.
- This is a scoped link-expansion trace, not a claim of exhaustive retrieval. Local `life_log_linker_regression.py`, `associative_retrieval_smoke.py`, and `git diff --check` passed. Native compilation is unavailable locally because the SQLite development headers are absent; exact-head native CI for this follow-up is pending.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-330 — Unreadable sound assets outrank playable candidates

- Traced `R2Sounds.c:r2_sounds_select_file` through filename scoring and the subsequent local player spawn. The selector verified a candidate was a regular file but did not verify the runtime user could read it. An unreadable exact-state clip could outrank a readable default clip; the player would then be given an inaccessible file, even though an eligible readable asset existed.
- Check effective-user read access during the existing candidate selection, before scoring. This preserves the established state ranking and only excludes assets that the playback user cannot read. The native sound fixture removes read permission from an exact match, checks the existing generic candidate is selected when access really is denied, restores permissions, and checks exact selection resumes. Root execution cannot exercise the denied-read assertion and is explicitly distinguished. No context/response allocations or model settings changed.
- Source trace and regression added; exact-head compilation and execution remain pending. File readability is not a guarantee that the MP3 decodes or the physical speaker plays it.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-331 — Memory decision can hide a line break around the category separator
