### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-332 — Snapshot refresh unnecessarily evicts complete dialogue

- Traced both foreground refresh calls in `r2_talk_impl` through `refresh_reality_context_message`, the live message pair admission planner and final context selection. The refresh first admitted two new system snapshots alongside the old Reality/learning snapshots, then removed duplicates. With a full byte or slot budget, the planner could evict a still-needed earlier exchange to make room for old and new snapshots simultaneously, even when the final replacement plus that exchange fit. It could also reject a replacement that would fit after superseded snapshots were removed. This is separate from the wire-token budget and can affect the next follow-up before inference starts.
- Added a mode to the existing atomic pair planner to account for the two superseded system snapshot classes before choosing any further evictions. The exact old snapshots are only released in the existing commit/compaction loop after all new copies and array capacity are reserved. All other callers use the unchanged ordinary pair semantics. Refresh uses the replacement mode; no duplicate cleanup pass is necessary. Snapshot classes match the original role/heading predicate; no persistent records, context cap, model settings, or generation budget change.
- Added native byte-pressure cases that require a complete old exchange to survive a fitting replacement and that verify a non-fitting replacement leaves the old state and dialogue untouched. Source-level review covered the ordinary pair wrappers, foreground callers, active-turn protection and relevant static test contracts; exact-head native and integration execution remain pending. Full recursive repository review remains open.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-25-334 — Active Hands evidence lacks the ownership of its delivery ID

- Traced `r2_talk_impl` from `attach_pending_hands_result` through initial answer, action-result pair admission, post-action Reality refresh, final answer admission, durable `r2_log_deliver_hands_result`, and outer cleanup. The durable pending outcome ID remains associated with the turn, but neither temporary message was protected by `ActiveTurnHistory`. Live-history pressure could evict the handoff during the turn while the ID remained eligible for delivery acknowledgement. Further, keeping the temporary pair through visible-reply admission needlessly consumed slots and bytes after inference was finished.
- Track both temporary content allocations in the existing active-turn ownership record, preserving the same ordinary pair admission and required inference selection. Release the temporary pair under messages_lock immediately before admitting the final visible reply, and perform idempotent cleanup under the same lock on early exits. The existing durable acknowledgement condition and database transaction are unchanged; no task execution, context ceiling, or model setting changes.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-336 — Valid first-person assistant output is classified as missing conversation

- Read `R2Reply.c` in full and traced both current core callers at `r2.c:6876` (association cues) and `7452` (foreground answer). `ollama_submit_chat` reads the assistant final content independently from the thinking field (lines 5262–5318). The sanitizer nevertheless treats an ordinary leading phrase such as "I'll answer that question in a moment." or "I need to answer the phone." as private scratch because `scratch_prefixes` contains broad first-person phrases (238–246, 412–439). Without a subsequent line beginning Final:, it frees the complete generated answer and returns NULL. `r2_talk_impl:15628–15647` then substitutes a hardcoded failure response which is appended to live dialogue at 15816–15823. This is a deterministic valid-answer rejection path, distinct from transport errors or a dropped input.
- Existing `tests/r2_reply_smoke.c` exercises explicitly labeled and third-person planning output, nested tags and Markdown, but not these ordinary direct first-person beginnings. Need distinguish ambiguous natural first-person language from actual private tags/labels and explicit planning boundaries without exposing real private-channel content. Preserve existing control/literal parsing and do not add a recovery/retry layer. Source correction deferred until the broader inspection phase is complete, as requested; no claim that this explains every reported second-turn failure.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-337 — CRLF reply fence leaves later sound control exposed

The latter accepts only spaces and tabs after closing ticks. A normal CRLF fenced example followed by an active sound marker therefore stays in fence mode, exposes the control text, and never schedules the effect. This reaches the final visible reply through `r2.c:15564–15586,15781–15799`.

The reply sanitizer already tests for a matching run. Add regressions for CRLF examples and an unmatched tick, while preserving literal fenced/inline sound markers and the existing final-only playback contract. No source changes yet.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-339 — Explicit diary operation can fail while post-turn learning owns inference

That function explicitly selected `ollama_background_mode=1`, but the shared gate only waits for a busy slot when `ollama_reflection_worker` is true. The manual caller is not that worker. On a successful diary write, request dispatch of the already-existing durable post-turn queue. The autonomous worker, 900-second reflection cadence, context allocation, database transaction and exact diary text remain unchanged. No new retries, generic output or alternate model were added.

This fixture needs full native execution.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-340 — Explicit reflection lifecycle and queue publication race

- Independently traced `r2_write_diary`, `r2_talk`, `resume_post_turn_learning`, `post_turn_scheduler`, `r2_shutdown` and the actual diary staging and Life Log completion transaction. The previous foreground model-gate fix did not serialize the entire explicit diary operation. A concurrent shutdown could close stores while manual context assembly or reflection persistence was still active; a replay sweep could also sample its cutoff before the reflection was staged, release the DB lock, and clear the newer replay flag after the manual caller set it. Both `diary` and `think` enter the same explicit operation, and neither already holds the conversation lock. Own the existing foreground counter and conversation lifecycle lock from entry through context assembly, model inference, durable reflection and replay notification; release both on every exit. No new inference or persistence path, timer change or response recovery. The autonomous worker is unchanged. Extend the real shutdown fixture to reject a new manual diary request after shutdown without dispatching inference or leaking the foreground/queue flags. The earlier stalled-learning manual diary fixture remains applicable.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-341 — Diary file-tool numeric and full-read correctness

- Read all 2,873 source lines of `r2_diary.c`, `r2_diary.h`, `Log.h`, `SensoryJournal.h`, and `tests/workspace_read_inode_runtime.c` in full; inspected `tests/diary_integration_smoke.c` 1–290 and `tests/inference_request_runtime.c` 1000–1108, 2640–2780. The diary full-file read uses `ftell` before `fread`, then returns that earlier byte count even when additional bytes arrive before reading. It also uses `rewind` without checking repositioning and ignores the close result. Correct the existing reader to check seek, exact byte count, EOF, stream error and close before publishing the full result. Keep binary low-level reads and existing 10 MiB limit unchanged; chunk reads retain their requested-length semantics and now check close. `strtoull` accepted negative chunk lengths, converting `-1` into a large unsigned count that was silently clamped to 10 MiB. Reject a negative sign (including after leading whitespace) at the existing numeric parser. Add fixtures for signed lengths, a file that grows between size measurement and read, complete subsequent read and empty file. No new filesystem capability or allocation cap.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-344 — Historical action evidence must not erase the preceding visible exchange

This is distinct from the active action result, which must remain required.

### Audit 345 — Startup transaction and line-local control parsing
- Life Log initialization previously published readiness before legacy relationship repair, while migration DDL and synthetic recovery events committed separately. Restoring a row whose UPDATE was reverted by a trigger could loop without progress. Schema, aliases, baseline and legacy event relationships now share one owned transaction under log_lock. Verify the repaired endpoint after UPDATE and distinguish failed schema introspection from absent columns. Foreign keys are enabled before the transaction; startup journal reconciliation reads committed authoritative events. Legacy records and IDs are preserved. Regression fixtures check rollback of failed DDL and a reverted event link, followed by a successful migration without orphan or duplicate events.
- Control parsing previously accepted a numeric argument from the next line via strtoll's implicit whitespace skipping. Single and paired positive IDs now start on the same line. HTML token scanning no longer mistakes longer tag names for pre/code delimiters or forms a pointer beyond the line. Native assertions cover these exact boundaries. Existing authorization and visible-response requirements are unchanged.

- Audit 344 follow-through: workflow 45930856943 reached the existing complete-exchange fixture and failed its unchanged assertion for an unmarked internal request. The new selection originally treated a tagged result as removable even if the adjacent request was not identifiable. Require every historical handoff to be a verified adjacent request/result pair before allowing visible-only selection. Preserve original all-or-nothing behavior for ambiguous handoffs, and retain the old regression assertions. Added positive paired and human-quoted-marker cases. This was a real regression in the first implementation, not a test to discard.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-348 — Observer replay cursor after live Activity.txt replacement

- Fully read `Observer.py` and `tests/observer_filter_smoke.py` (56/192 cumulative full-file reads using previous ledger count). Traced ActivityWriter inode/offset cache, authoritative read-only Life Log paging, Tk poll cursor, and popup pruning. The file identity was reset only inside append, after SQL had already skipped earlier IDs. A renamed, deleted or shortened Activity file could therefore omit all earlier public events until process restart; if no new events arrived, it would not be rebuilt at all.

A change detected inside append during a page rejects the page without writing newer rows out of order; the existing poll error path retains its cursor. Advance the independent display high-water after each newly displayed event so rebuilding a file cannot reinsert pruned historical popup rows. Preserve event allowlist, read-only database, exact event IDs and original record bytes. Add native Python fixtures covering live rename, truncation, bounded replay and replacement between selection and append; no new daemon, recovery inference or changes to R2 chat.
- Local git clone failed DNS resolution. Source/test change made through the GitHub connection, not the physical host. Exact-head test execution pending; no claim of repository-wide completeness. In-place edits preserving inode and file length cannot be detected by this existing inode/size approach, and arbitrary concurrent noncooperating writes remain outside the file-lock contract.

- Recursive impact review of 348: the original cursor reset could itself be lost if the first replacement page failed before returning a new cursor to the Tk caller. Keep the rewind pending until a full page completes; also mark a mid-page inode change pending. Test both first-write failure and partial-page failure, requiring the exact historical bytes after retry. This extends the existing cursor ownership rather than introducing a new retry mechanism.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-353 — Failed dialogue must not become the next turn's assistant evidence

- The live finalizer unconditionally appended its display fallback as an assistant message. A failed plain dialogue could therefore appear as R2's previous answer in the very next request, even though no model answer existed. Do not append a non-generated diagnostic when there is no completed tool evidence. Preserve the exact unanswered user turn. Update the associative planner's previous-exchange discovery, optional reply framing, complete byte reservation and non-generative search terms to retain a preceding user-only turn (including when the planner itself returns invalid JSON). This does not change context tiers or truncate user messages. Test actual marker-only failed reply, following user turn, wire roles and previous unanswered user message through successful and failed planning. Existing completed-action visible-result continuity is preserved deliberately, because it contains verified action evidence and has a separate tight-history admission contract; attribution of that live wrapper still needs a dedicated change. Persistent attribution is already corrected by audit 352. No full-file count increase for scoped core reads.

### R2-AUD-2026-11-26-354 — Decode output geometry rather than coded stream geometry

- Exact-head workflow 45935660042 for 5771395 passed 60 steps, including the native inference and complete persistent-context fixtures, then failed a source-text test that used the removed retry enum as an arbitrary slice delimiter. Anchor that test to the actual following submit declaration; keep every allocator assertion intact. Local cloning remains DNS-blocked.
- Reproduced actual rotated file geometry with FFmpeg: first track 16x32 red, second 64x32 blue, both without default dispositions. A 90-degree (and locally 89.5-degree) display matrix swaps the decoded raster to 32x16 while the coded stream stays 16x32. FFprobe's reported rotation is integer-rounded, so matching only metadata angles can still be wrong near the decoder's quarter-turn threshold. Use a bounded one-frame FFmpeg framecrc probe with the exact playback stream map, RGB24 filter and rawvideo codec; require one decoded packet whose byte length equals the reported dimensions times three. The normal decoder still streams raw RGB frames with no resolution scaling, 10 fps and original autorotation. No changes to physical camera or X11 window inputs. The probe decodes one additional frame at open rather than just reading headers; its existing five-second deadline applies, and a source that cannot decode a frame in that budget will not open. Midstream format changes remain a separate concern; no claim of handling arbitrary dynamic geometry.
- Updated fake decoder fixtures for actual packet geometry, no-frame and wrong-size rejection, and rotated native frame boundaries. Add real FFmpeg regression in CI covering ordinary and rotated two-track media, expected dimensions/stride/size, red pixels (not the larger blue track), and exact frame count. Local real FFmpeg command reproduction for base and rotated 89.5-degree samples yielded 1536-byte first packets, 4608 RGB bytes across three frames, correct red pixels; full repo test not locally built. No physical host changes. Scoped reinspection only, no coverage-count increase.

### 357. Preserve shared action evidence during post-turn job allocation

- Inspected receipt ownership from `recorded_action_reply` through durable conversation insertion, post-turn scheduling, worker cleanup and pending-learning replay. If receipt formatting fails, the reply aliases the original evidence. Previously the caller duplicated it inline without testing allocation success, allowing a worker to acknowledge a staged row after learning from an incomplete request.
- Scheduler now explicitly distinguishes borrowed and owned evidence. It copies borrowed evidence only when admitting a live worker and rejects incomplete worker allocation, leaving the already committed full evidence pending. Busy/shutdown/allocation paths never free the borrowed reply. No conversational retry, regenerated response, or new action execution.

### 365. Keep historical exchanges intact during live-history admission
